# Research Queue (claim an idea by moving it to "In progress" with the date; results go to LEADERBOARD.md)
## Open
315. does-de-lagging-the-breadth-gate-turn-it-into-a-real-bear-defence — idea 48's by-product (breadth cash gate at the causal bottom quintile) is the grid's highest-Sharpe non-live book (12.2%/1.131/-12.7%, OOS 1.240) yet returns -9.0% in 2022 because the flag fires in February, after January's loss, and stays on through the Q4 recovery. Test the LAG directly: gate on E_t's own rate of change (E_t vs its 20d mean) instead of its level, and on a re-entry that requires the level to clear the quantile rather than fall below it. If the 2022 column moves, the gate is a timing signal; if it does not, it is a volatility discount and belongs in RULES as an exposure clause, not a defence. Max 2 params. (2026-09-07)
316. is-CONCENTRATION-in-narrow-markets-a-general-clause-or-one-2022 — idea 48 found the fraction leg's only real work is concentrating into fewer names when breadth collapses, worth ~0.6 pp/yr on 13.3% of days and bought with 3.2 pp of drawdown, on effectively ONE episode (2022 is the only year where the narrow flag is on almost continuously). Re-test the narrow-day concentration effect on universe_broad.json and the SMALL439 panel, where narrow episodes are more numerous, and report whether the sign survives outside 2022. If it does not, no fraction clause of any form belongs in RULES. Max 2 params. (2026-09-07)
317. do-the-record-s-CONDITIONAL-clauses-ever-beat-their-own-unconditional-parents — idea 48's decisive test was not 4a/4b but 'does the conditional rule beat BOTH the rules it interpolates' (answer 4/16 full-sample, 0/16 on drawdown). Audit every committed conditional/regime-switching book on the LEADERBOARD against that bar with its own two parents run at matched gross. If conditional clauses systematically fail it, the bar belongs in PROTOCOL rule 4 as a precondition for KEEP, ahead of the half-sample tests. (2026-09-07)
314. is-the-vol-cap-the-real-universe-clause-not-the-trend-leg — idea 51R splits the RULES v1 eligibility filter into its legs and finds the vol20 < 0.60 cap costs SMALL439 -3.93 pp/yr (dSharpe -0.204) against the 200d trend leg's -1.44 pp/yr (-0.074), while on U56/B136 the two legs are comparable. Sweep the cap LEVEL (0.40/0.60/0.80/1.00/off) against the trend leg held fixed on all three panels at matched gross, and report whether a panel-scaled cap (e.g. a cross-sectional vol quantile instead of an absolute 0.60) removes the small-cap damage — i.e. whether any universe clause belongs in RULES at all, and phrased in volatility rather than capitalisation. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
311. does-4b-discriminate-ANYTHING-on-gross-scalar-books — idea 51 solved the admissible 4b gross band for 30 unlevered book-forms and found it non-empty in 20 and passing in 20/20, the no-filter EWall control included, because Sharpe is invariant in g (span <= 0.0050) while CAGR and MaxDD scale ~linearly. Re-read the record's 4b passes: how many are gross-scalar books whose verdict is a dial placement rather than an edge, and should PROTOCOL 4b quote the admissible g-BAND instead of a ladder point? Max 2 params.
312. is-the-panel-ordering-an-ETF-SHARE-effect — idea 51's gate premium is monotone U56 > B136 > SMALL439 and survives rule 8, but U56 is 36/56 ETFs and SMALL439 is 0% ETF. Idea 277 already priced ETF dilution at R2 0.0041. Hold ETF share fixed by building matched sub-panels at 0%, 25% and 50% ETF from B136 and re-measuring dSharpe of MA-RS vs EWall. If the ordering flattens, the 'universe boundary' is an asset-class boundary.
313. why-do-capQ-and-advQ-disagree-in-SIGN — idea 51's two size columns give decile slopes of -0.336 and +0.335 for the same book on the same panel, correlating only +0.545. Decompose: is the disagreement the static-vs-dynamic membership, the look-ahead in the 2026 cap stamp, or survivorship in the thin deciles (advQ decile 1 EWall Sharpe 2.03)? Rebuild capQ as a causal price-times-fixed-shares proxy and re-read both slopes.
305. does-the-uncompensated-residual-finding-replicate-off-SMALL439 — idea 300 priced the MA gate against a matched QUANTILE gate on SMALL439 only and found the extra 0.38 pp/yr of residual buys dSharpe -0.0092 (OOS -0.0256). Run the identical matched design on U56 and B136, where idea 298 measured the same residual gap, and report whether dSharpe stays <= 0 at c_bar >= 0.5 on both. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
306. is-the-MA-threshold-edge-a-CONSTANT-GROSS-effect — idea 300's attribution says the MA form wins +1.09 pp/yr and +0.048 Sharpe in RESPREAD (exposure pinned at 1) and loses all of it under de-grossing. Test the RESPREAD MA-threshold book directly against the matched constant-depth book across cadence and gross in {0.50, 0.75, 1.00} and report whether the selection edge survives costs at any gross. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
307. does-the-QUANTILE-zero-residual-hold-at-DAILY-and-ANNUAL-cadence — idea 300 showed the QUANTILE gate's zero timing residual walks forward (OOS -0.0002 pp/yr, MAE 0.0216) at W/M/Q on SMALL439, which is what makes it usable as a control. Push the cadence dial to D and A and report whether the zero survives the extremes, since a control that only holds at three cadences is not a control. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
308. why-does-the-CORR-ordering-die-at-q-0.75 — idea 293 measured corr rho_within -0.343/-0.315/-0.146 at q=0.25/0.50/0.75, with only 1 of 9 significant and seed halves disagreeing in 5 of 9 at q=0.75, yet k=80 is the one width where q=0.75 survives (-0.21..-0.27). Test the two candidate causes at fixed q=0.75: shrinking cross-panel corr SPREAD (a range restriction) versus a rising OOS Sharpe noise floor, by reporting the corr sd and the seed sd of OOS Sharpe per stratum. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
309. does-S_CORR-beat-the-anchor-once-the-PANEL-is-controlled — idea 293's S_CORR beats a random draw in 15/18 cells, but its only 4b-clearing pick sits on a panel whose un-ranked EWall book and RULES v2 also clear 4b. Re-score every selector on the EWall-RESIDUAL of OOS Sharpe (book OOS Sharpe minus that panel's EWall OOS Sharpe) so a selector is credited only for picking a panel the RANKING exploits, and report which selectors survive. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
310. is-EVOL-the-real-survivor-not-DISP — idea 284 (one stratum) found disp survives the partial control and evol collapses; idea 293 (nine strata) found the reverse: mean partial evol +0.1551 vs disp +0.0046 across 27 points. Re-fit both partials stratum by stratum and report which of the two is stable in sign and size, and whether the disagreement is the k=40/q=0.5 cell alone. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
299. pre-register-the-quantile-family-as-a-dial — idea 298's PARKed by-product (U56 top-50%-above-MA200, equal weight, monthly, 75% gross) clears 4b at 15.53%/1.2400/-19.80% (OOS 1.2237) but needed a third dial. Re-run with family and x as THE two pre-registered dials at the record's weekly default, and require the 4b pass on U56 AND B136 with MaxDD clearing the cap by >1pp. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
301. is-the-gate-timing-residual-a-constant-in-pp-per-year — idea 298 replaced the share curve with a zero-parameter discount: subtract the gate's own timing residual, which fitted flat in c_bar (intercept -0.619/-0.267/-0.352 pp/yr on the three MA arms, slope R2 0.04-0.19). Test the constancy directly: estimate the residual on IS cells per (panel, family) and predict OOS residual in pp/yr against the naive zero and the IS mean, reporting whether a per-panel constant beats a single global one. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
295. restate-the-26-characteristic-files-under-the-SIGN-FLIP — idea 276 counted 26 files (14 tight) attributing a result to a panel property across the cap line; idea 284 shows breadth's within-stratum content is zero and disp/corr/evol reverse sign under the control. Re-read each of those files' headline and classify it as (a) zero once controlled, (b) sign-reversed once controlled, or (c) already within-stratum; report how many published direction claims survive. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
285. is-the-4b-footprint-a-monotone-function-of-cap-mix — idea 276's 4b passes were 3/66 (n=10) and 16/66 (n=20), every one at q<=0.5, i.e. the KEEP path's own eligibility tracks the panel's capitalisation. Sweep q at finer resolution with more draws and report the q at which each 4b bar (H1, H2, OOS, DD cap, CAGR floor) first binds — a published admissibility curve rather than a per-panel verdict. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
286. price-the-14-breadth-files-on-their-own-books — idea 276's census leaves 14 files naming `breadth` explicitly as the tight lower bound on exposure. Re-run each of those files' headline comparison with the cap mix q reported beside the breadth reading, and count how many headlines survive the restatement. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
290. how-many-published-panel-shares-are-7-cell-binomials — idea 277 found the within-panel seed sd of the matched-ratio reversal share is 0.2641 against a mechanical 7-cell floor of 0.1890, and that ETF36's published 2/7 is an ordinary draw from k-matched STOCK panels. Census every published claim in the record that ranks or contrasts panels on a share computed from <= 10 cells, attach each one's binomial sd, and report how many orderings survive their own sampling noise. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
291. is-the-ETF-dilution-slope-a-BETA-story-or-a-BREADTH-story — idea 277 found EWall OOS Sharpe falls 1.0597 -> 0.6656 monotonically in 8/8 steps as the ETF share goes 0 -> 1 at fixed k=36, while breadth is FLAT (0.6821 -> 0.6750) and dispersion halves (0.0964 -> 0.0620). Decompose the slope into the mean-return, vol and cross-sectional-dispersion channels at matched realised gross, and report which one carries it. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
292. does-the-suppression-pattern-appear-under-other-panel-dials — idea 277 found the ETF share is worth R2 0.0041 marginally but t +3.47 conditional on idea 271's four characteristics, with evol's coefficient flipping -0.2058 -> +0.3222. Re-fit every published panel-characteristic regression in the record with and without its own construction dial and report how many coefficients change sign, i.e. how much of the record's characteristic story is suppression rather than effect. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
278. do-CLASSIFIER-selectors-behave-differently-from-ARGMAX-selectors — idea 271's CSEL is one of the few selectors in the record that BEATS do-nothing out of sample (+0.0476 of Sharpe over EWALL) while LOSING on the full sample, the opposite shape to the argmax choosers in idea 229's pool. Re-classify every selector in that pool by whether it maximises an IS metric or thresholds a fitted probability, and test whether the two classes have different OOS expectancy. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)

283. does-the-no-signal-shape-appear-on-any-panel-that-passes — idea 60's saturation test is two numbers off any band ladder (the 0.00->0.03 increment vs the 0.03->widest increment) and it separates 'the gate loses on noise crossings' from 'the gate loses on every crossing'. Back-fill it over idea 61's 408 cells and idea 57's 40, and report whether the arms that clear 4b are exactly the ones whose recovery curve SATURATES. If the diagnostic separates them it is a screening column; if not it is a KILL. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
275. price-the-removal-of-the-daily-hard-exit — RULES v2 dropped v1's 'hard exit any day' clause because no backtest in the record ever priced it: every book applies weights on its rebalance schedule only. Build the daily-exit variant of both v1 and v2 (sell at the next close whenever the gate flips, buy back only on schedule) and report what the clause was actually worth in Sharpe, MaxDD and turnover across 2009-2026. This is a live-rules fidelity question, not a search for an edge. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
272. hold-the-ratio-fixed-DAY-BY-DAY-not-at-the-median — idea 269C set n = round(r*median n_elig), but n_elig moves 18 -> 48 on U56 and 48 -> 116 on B136 across the sample, so realised r drifts and sat_share reaches 0.52 at r=1. Re-run the matched-ratio grid with a time-varying n_t = round(r * n_elig,t) so the ratio is genuinely held fixed, and report whether the non-monotonicity and the panel disagreement survive. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
270. is-S_CAGR-vs-S_SHARPE-a-general-selector-pair — idea 259 found the two IS selectors pick a DIFFERENT arm in 3 of 4 panels on the n dial, worth +2.53 pp/yr of OOS CAGR for -0.0254 of OOS Sharpe. Run the same selector pair over the record's OTHER swept dials (cadence, gross, vol gate, eligibility trim) wherever the parent committed a grid CSV with IS_CAGR, and report whether the +CAGR/-Sharpe exchange rate is a constant or a dial-specific one. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
259. does-the-sharpe-cagr-reversal-sit-under-every-EWall-claim — idea 82's headline reverses sign between Sharpe and CAGR at t +4.03 and t +4.93 on the SAME eight cells, and the record quotes EWall-beats-ranked on Sharpe in several places (ideas 10, 72, 73, 77, 82). Back-fill the CAGR column beside every published EWall-vs-ranked comparison whose parent committed a grid CSV, and report how many are Sharpe-only verdicts that reverse. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
255. does-ddctl-really-own-the-deep-budgets — idea 251 found that back-filling reach flips 4 of 40 published menu picks, all the same way: idea 40's book DD control displaces de-gross at T=8-10pp (0.43-0.51 vs 0.58-0.61 pp/pp) once ddctl's ladder runs to 2%. That is a cheaper-than-the-lever claim at the deepest budgets, made on a 4-cell median. Test it directly: ddctl in {0.02..0.40} against the matched-realised-gross static control (idea 244's channel) on u56/B136/SMALL484, and report whether the deep-budget win survives gross matching and rule 8. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
257. window-stamp-every-reach-and-every-depth-denominator — idea 251 found OOS reach exceeds IS reach in 47 of 48 cells (stop 0.80 -> 5.14pp, ddctl 4.05 -> 9.89pp), i.e. any statistic normalised by 'drawdown available to buy' is a function of the window's crashes, not of the instrument. Test whether re-quoting the record's exchange rates per crisis episode at matched SPY depth (idea 117's denominator) removes the IS/OOS gap. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
252. name-level-fixed-effects-instead-of-a-draw-scalar — idea 83's control is ONE scalar per draw (mean, plus max as a second control), and dispersion survived it at t +5.87 with 62% of its R2 intact. The stronger design is name-level: regress each draw's Sharpe on its 136-column membership indicator matrix (ridge, k<<136 so it is rank-deficient by construction) and ask what dispersion adds to the FITTED composition effect, not to a summary of it. If sd still survives, the 62% is structural and idea 78's diagnostics are safe outright; if it dies, idea 83's answer is a power result about the scalar, not about dispersion. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06, lane B)
253. does-a-random-sub-panel-pass-4b-because-the-CAGR-floor-moves — idea 83 reproduced idea 78's finding that random k=80 sub-panels of B136 clear 4b at a 46% base rate, and its own census puts the binding bars at H2 233 / DD 203 / CAGR 194 of 450. A 46% base rate means the bar admits noise on this panel at this k. Back-fill, for every 4b pass in the record, which bar was CLOSEST to binding and by how much, and report how many published 4b passes sit inside their own panel's random-sub-panel base rate. Bears on every 4b count the project has published. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06, lane B)
247. fix-hivol80s-IS-OOS-coverage-and-re-read-the-parked-arm — idea 246 PARKed `EWall + band3-dg armed only in hivol80` (u56 @10bps 12.2%/1.21/-15.5%, halves 1.23/1.19, OOS 1.2831, 4b PASS on u56 AND broad at both rungs, its always-on sibling FAILS) for one reason: the expanding 80th-pct vol regime arms 3.3% of IS days against 17.1% of OOS days, so rule 8 selected it on ~66 armed IS days. Re-run the arm with a FIXED pre-registered vol threshold chosen so IS and OOS coverage match, plus a matched-realised-gross static control (idea 244's channel), and report whether the 4b pass survives. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
248. is-LEAK-a-general-tax-on-every-published-per-armed-day-number — idea 246 found the record's `per armed day` normalisation is 52% LEAK (what the arm does while DISARMED, multiplied by (1-f)/f) against 13% for the effect it is named after, and that the same comparison reverses sign unnormalised. (2026-09-06)
249. do-de-grossing-instruments-belong-armed-only-in-crashes-after-all — idea 246 found the sign idea 75 reported for stops runs the OTHER way for pure exposure cuts: `gross50` and `ddctl8` have always-on delta +12.6 and +5.8 pp/yr ON crash days against -9.2 and -4.6 OFF them, i.e. the crash regime is the one regime they pay in. Re-price both at matched realised gross with the switching cost (switch_mult 5.9-11.6x) charged explicitly, and report whether either is cheap enough to survive once LEAK is not divided out. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)

244. how-many-published-count-dials-are-gross-dials — idea 240 found idea 73's fixed GROSS/n weight de-grosses a wide book without saying so (U56 n=60 realised gross 0.474 vs 0.721 at n=20) and that closing the channel halves the width premium and flips two cells. 239. publish-EWall-beside-every-panel-claim — idea 77 found the un-ranked `EWall` Sharpe of a panel predicts its ranked book's OOS Sharpe BETTER than the ranked book's own IS Sharpe (+0.857 p 0.024 vs +0.821 p 0.034). 237. the-dg-reachability-ceiling — idea 154 found idea 84's g=0.85 is not an inadmissible arm under the `dg` convention but an UNDEFINED one: the de-grossed band3 book tops out at realised gross 0.6924 at m=1.30. 238. band3-rw-at-075-vs-the-incumbent — idea 154 found `EWall + band3-rw` at g=0.75 is 6 of 6 large-cap cells with a WIDER broad drawdown margin than the incumbent (+0.0170 vs +0.0083 at 10 bps) but lower broad CAGR and Sharpe. 233. does-turnover-level-predict-chooser-power — pool idea 230's 33 cells with idea 228's 12 and regress `cost_of_0bps_pick_at_30` on the do-nothing book's turnover; if it is a clean function of turnover, the cost ladder's chooser value is publishable from one number instead of a census. 235. is-selection-beats-do-nothing-just-a-rung-artefact — idea 230's rule-8 premium is monotone in the cost rung for BOTH the IS chooser (-0.0161 at 0 bps -> +0.1055 at 30) and a RANDOM dial (-0.0275 -> +0.0076), and is a coin flip at PROTOCOL's own 10 bps. 231. the-gap-vs-tilt-rule-as-a-leaderboard-column — idea 228's re-rank predictor (0-bps top-2 Sharpe gap vs the ladder's 30-bp tilt) catches 3/3 actual re-ranks and over-predicts 3 cells. 227. is-the-top-decile-trim-a-drop-the-worst-rule — idea 155's only surviving edge is q=0.90-0.95, i.e. deleting the bottom 5-10% of the eligible set, worth +0.0316/+0.0171 Sharpe. 229. the-tenth-selection-loses-instance-as-a-distribution — idea 155 is the 10th time an IS chooser lost to doing nothing OOS. 224. does-modal-share-predict-VARIANCE-not-sign — idea 219 killed the share floor on the MEAN but its curve shape (d ~ 0 at share >= 0.95 by construction, largest spread at low share) says share bounds how far a mode can move the answer. Fit sd(d) rather than mean(d) against share across the 560 cells and test whether a share-scaled CONFIDENCE column, not a gate, is the reportable statistic. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06)
225. back-fill-the-split-half-distribution-over-every-single-draw-claim — idea 219 showed a published split-half verdict (idea 189's N cell, -0.0229) flips sign when the ONE seeded split becomes 80. 226. why-the-035-045-share-window-dips — idea 219's local curve is positive everywhere except a -0.003 wobble at share 0.30-0.42 whose 90% bootstrap CI reaches the grid edge. Test whether that window is a dial-composition artefact (it is where BAND+ and SLEEVE+ concentrate) or a real hole, by re-running the curve dial-by-dial and with each dial view held out in turn. 214. does-the-q95-band-hold-its-nominal-5-percent — idea 207 recommends replacing clause 11b's `max` with a Q95 band at 100 draws, but a quantile's NOMINAL size is only its REALISED size if the draws are exchangeable, and neighbouring circular rotations are correlated. Run idea 191's known-null NOISE arm (episodic Bernoulli, zero information) through the Q95/K=100 band on all three panels and report the realised false-positive rate against 5%. If it is not 5%, clause 11b needs a calibrated quantile, not a nominal one. Max 2 params. (2026-09-05)
215. back-fill-the-q95-band-over-every-committed-null-claim — idea 207 shows the MAX band at K=20 and a Q95 band at K=100 disagree on 26.7% of configurations. Re-read every committed rotation-null claim (ideas 181/186/191/192/201) under Q95/K=100 wherever the parent's grid CSV survives, and count how many published `clears` verdicts move and in which direction. The output is the size of the record's exposure to the statistic it happened to pick. Cheap; max 2 params. (2026-09-05)
216. a-flip-rate-estimator-that-is-not-confounded-by-block-count — idea 207's flip rate at K=200 rests on ONE disjoint block pair per configuration and is the noisiest row in its table (Q95's zone widens 100 -> 200 while its band sd keeps falling). Build a paired estimator that holds the number of comparisons fixed across K — e.g. B bootstrap block pairs at every K from a larger pool — and re-run the ladder, so the K recommendation rests on an estimator whose variance does not itself depend on K. Max 2 params. (2026-09-05)
203. a-turnover-matched-null-for-suppressing-overlays — idea 191 measured the rotation null's turnover fidelity on BUDGET-skip at 1782.7% mean on a widened threshold grid (idea 186 published 25.4%/213.8%), i.e. the null is not turnover-matched for an overlay that suppresses a trade and degrades without bound in on-share. Build and validate a null that resamples SKIP DECISIONS at matched count rather than rotating the state, and re-price idea 186's BUDGET family against it. Max 2 params. (2026-09-05)

48. breadth-adaptive-count-2022 — **KILL of the conditional clause (2026-09-07, lane B).** HYB(q,f): fixed n0=20 when breadth is broad, top ceil(f*E_t) when E_t is below its own CAUSAL expanding q-quantile; 16 points (q 0.10/0.20/0.30/0.40 x f 0.25/0.35/0.45/0.55) + 8 unconditional controls + 3 narrow-regime treatments, 10 bps, weekly, t+1, all reported. **Premise holds**: the causal flag fires on 91% of 2022 (mean E_t 16.4 vs 37.5), 13.3% of days overall, 93.4% agreement with a full-sample quantile. **(1)** The clause loses to its own broad leg: HYB beats NF20 on full-sample Sharpe 4/16 (mean -0.011) and is DEEPER in drawdown 16/16 (mean -3.2 pp); it beats its narrow-leg parent F<f> 16/16 (+0.042), which only restates idea 46. **(2) Rule 8 does not pick the defence**: (q,f) chosen on 2009-2016 gives q0.40/f0.25 (OOS 16.7%/1.153/-25.0%) and q0.10/f0.25 (16.3%/1.185/-25.6%), BOTH failing 4b on the -20.2% DD cap while the unconditional N20 (14.4%/1.168/-18.3%) and F0.85 (12.4%/1.130/-16.7%) pass; the S2 pick's 2022 is -5.1%, not the +1.5% the idea was built on. **(3)** On universe_broad.json: 4a 0/16, 4b 0/16, 0/16 beat NF20 on Sharpe, every point failing H2+OOS+DD — the clause reverses sign on a universe change. **(4) The mechanism is CONCENTRATION, not timing**: at q=0.20 the cash and half-gross treatments both return -9.0% in 2022, identical to no gate, because the breadth flag LAGS (realised gross 0.52 in Jan 2022, 0.00 Feb-Nov) — it eats January at full exposure and misses the Q4 recovery; only the f=0.35 concentration arm gets +1.5%, and it is the only one that deepens drawdown (-21.5% vs -18.3%). Attribution: on the 591 narrow days the book earns -24.4%/yr under NF20 vs -20.2%/yr under HYB = ~0.6 pp/yr bought with 3.2 pp of drawdown. Grid 4a 0/27, 4b 14/27 (HYB 0/16 and 7/16), every 4b pass matched by a control the record already owns. **By-product**: the same flag as a plain CASH gate on NF20 is the grid's highest-Sharpe non-live book (12.2%/1.131/-12.7%, OOS 1.240, one parameter, 4b pass / 4a fail) but is NOT a bear defence (2022 -9.0%) — queued as 315. No memo, RULES unchanged. Script `2026-09-07_breadth-adaptive-count-2022_B.py`.

The lookback dial agrees (SMALL439 dSharpe -0.396 / -0.340 / -0.291 at lb 100/200/300, weekly): a slower trend leg is a less-bad trend leg, never a good one. **The queue's question is aimed at the cheaper of the two legs.** **(3)** No size gradient and no boundary: rho(decile, dSharpe) -0.127 (CAP, t -0.36) and -0.042 (ADV, t -0.12), 0 of 10 deciles positive on either proxy, CAGR slopes disagreeing in sign (-0.176 vs +0.430) — lane B's capQ/advQ split reproduced on an independent decile grid. **(4)** Rule 8: 1/18 picks beat the matched no-filter control OOS, 6/18 beat SPY, 0/18 beat the live book; sweep 4a 0/126, 4b 17/126 with **12 of the 17 passers still losing to their own control** and 3 of the 5 winners being the VOL-only book; deciles 4a 1/80 (rejected: control returns 19.71% CAGR, least-liquid survivorship decile), 4b 0/80. 51. trend-filter-by-market-cap — **ANSWERED; KILL of the market-cap reading. The universe boundary is REAL and survives rule 8, but it is NOT a cap boundary and no RULES clause phrased in market cap is supportable** (2026-09-06, lane B). The filter ALONE (EW-eligible vs EW-all, no ranking, matched gross) over 3 panels x 5 arms x 3 gross x 2 cadence = 90 books plus a 60-book decile grid. **(1)** The gate costs CAGR in 72/72 filter cells and Sharpe in 54/72; mean dSharpe is monotone across panels in all 4 arms — U56 -0.004 (MA-RS) / +0.062 (MA-DG), B136 -0.047 / -0.009, SMALL439 -0.102 / -0.082 (MAVOL-RS -0.269). **(2)** Idea 49's -5.4 pp/yr REPRODUCES to 0.1 pp at its own 0-bps rung (SMALL439/MAVOL-RS -5.31 pp), but ~60% of the headline damage on every panel is the DE-GROSSING convention, not selection (U56 MA-RS -1.21% vs MA-DG -4.72%). **(3)** The cap leg KILLS the cap reading: two independent size columns give opposite-signed decile slopes (capQ -0.336 vs advQ +0.335, series corr only +0.545) and the gate is negative in 19/20 decile cells under each, at 0 and 10 bps. **(4)** Rule 8 leaves the panel ordering intact (dOOS Sharpe +0.124 U56 / +0.053 B136 / -0.045 SMALL439, MAVOL arms -0.279/-0.301), IS chooser at the grid edge g=1.00 in all 15 arms. **(5) By-product for PROTOCOL:** Sharpe span across g is <= 0.0050 in all 30 book-forms while CAGR scales 1.969x and MaxDD 1.902x, so 4b is two LEVEL tests on one dial — the admissible g-band is non-empty in 20/30 forms and 4b passes at the solved g* in 20/20, INCLUDING the no-filter EWall control (U56 g*=0.64 11.27%/1.124/-19.41%). Path 4b does not discriminate the filter from no filter; for a gross-scalar book it collapses to Calmar >= 0.527, and that line falls BETWEEN the panels (U56 0.589-0.683, B136 0.558-0.617, SMALL439 0.092-0.282), not inside them. 4a 1/90, 4b 10/90 on the fixed ladder; decile grid 4a 1/60, 4b 0/60. No memo, RULES unchanged.
304. does-the-EXPOSURE-vs-TIMING-split-price-the-live-de-gross-clause-correctly — **ANSWERED / KILL of the mispricing hypothesis: the live de-gross clause is priced CORRECTLY (2026-09-06, lane C).** 442-point ladder (2 panels x 6 bands x 17 gross rungs x DEGROSS/RESPREAD/EWALL) + 18 risk-matching bisections + rule-8 walk-forward, 10 bps, weekly, t+1. **B0**: `DEGROSS(0.03,0.75)` reproduces `baseline.rules_v2_weights` **weight-for-weight at 0.000e+00** and idea 297's live row to 4.9e-05, twin to 4.8e-05, c_bar 0.7101 to 5.0e-06 (deviation logged: idea 297's stricter live mask gives 2.941e-04 and FAILS the 1e-12 bar; this script uses the live book's own N = instruments priced that day. universe.json holds SPY as an instrument, so all arms keep it; dropping it gives 8.68%/1.2128/-11.90% and would not reproduce 297). **H1 PASS**: MaxDD weakly monotone in gross on **26/26** arms (0 violations); the RESPREAD twin matches the live -12.0549% at **g\* = 0.5002** (err -2.2e-08, no leverage). **H2 FAIL on all three clauses — the headline**: at matched drawdown the static twin is **8.14%/1.1608** vs live **8.66%/1.2056**, dCAGR **-0.52 pp/yr**, dSharpe **-0.0448**, and the live book wins BOTH halves (1.2259/1.1908 vs 1.2097/1.1288). dCAGR is negative in **12/12** matched (panel x band) cells (U56 -0.26..-1.39, B136 -0.46..-1.17), dSharpe in 9/12, and the sign is unchanged under a second matching target (annualised vol: U56 -0.35 pp/-0.0448, B136 -0.29 pp/-0.0373). **H3**: the three-way at identical -12.05% decomposes the live book's return at constant risk as **EWALL(g\*=0.385) 6.77% -> +1.37 pp the 200d band (RESPREAD g\*=0.500, 8.14%) -> +0.52 pp the dynamic de-gross (8.66%)**; the ungated static control is 1.89 pp/yr and 0.083 of Sharpe behind the live book at the same drawdown. So idea 297's **'96% of the CAGR gap is cash drag' is an ACCOUNTING SHARE, not a verdict** — the drag is not free, because removing it and re-matching risk statically gives back less than it saves. **H4 rule 8** (band by IS Sharpe, gross matched to the live book's IS MaxDD -7.89% on 2009-2016; 2017+ read once): on U56 the live book beats **all three** re-selected matched-risk alternatives on OOS Sharpe (**1.2851** vs RESPREAD 1.2665, DEGROSS 1.1728, EWALL 1.1359) at the shallowest OOS drawdown (-12.05% vs -13.31/-13.49/-14.33); on B136 the RESPREAD pick edges the transplanted live form (1.1632 vs 1.1185) only by carrying 2.3 pp more OOS drawdown than its IS match promised. All arms beat SPY OOS (0.8820); none clears 4b. **4a 11/442, 4b 54/442, no KEEP**: all 11 4a passers are one arm (RESPREAD b=0.12 at gross 0.20-0.45, CAGR 3.6-8.3%) that passes only because 4a's drawdown clause rewards holding less, and every one fails 4b on CAGR; binding-bar census CAGR 317 / DD 71 / pass 54, and the live book itself fails 4b on CAGR. (2026-09-04, lane B) — **ANSWERED / MECHANISM CONFIRMED / KILL as a rule (2026-09-06, lane B): whipsaw IS the mechanism — the 200d band is a near-pure churn dial (gate flips 8.91 → 1.20 per name per year, 7.4×, while mean exposure moves only 49.6% → 51.3%) and it removes 76% of the 200d gate's timing cost at weekly cadence (2.82 → 0.69pp) and ALL of it at monthly/quarterly (−0.43pp at M b=0.12, −0.52pp at Q b=0.08). But the recovery is confined to one corner: the vol20 half is unrecoverable at all 18 band×cadence cells (2.02–5.35pp) and DE-GROSSING is unrecoverable (4.78–8.33pp, barely moves with b) because there the cost is exposure, not churn. Pre-registered test: only **4 of 72** points beat the matched-cadence no-filter control on both CAGR and Sharpe, best margin **+0.053 Sharpe / +0.33pp CAGR** — the filter merely STOPS LOSING. **4a 0/72, 4b 0/72** (the no-filter control fails 4b too); best cell RESPREAD/MA/b=0.08/Q reads 10.5%/0.735/−36.6%. Rule 8: the dial walks forward (IS chooser picks a large band and a slow cadence in 4/4 arms, 3 of 6 picks beat the weekly control OOS) but every pick loses to SPY OOS (0.882) and to the live book OOS (1.285). BY-PRODUCT: turnover 13.4× → 2.2×; the hard weekly filter goes NEGATIVE at 50 bps (−1.8%/−0.064) while the banded quarterly one returns 9.5%/0.675 — any small-cap trend book should be banded and slow on cost grounds alone. Not a PARK: there is no candidate to park, only a mechanism finding.**

Separation panel (32 cells): holding the gate identical and slowing only the TRADES is **+0.0370** (15/16), while slowing only the gate's SAMPLING is **-0.0176** (2/16) — at matched flip rate the band's own contribution is **+0.0152** (6/8), so hysteresis is a real but minor instrument and the trade schedule is the real half. Flip rate is not a design variable (Spearman -1.0..+1.0, no stable sign; idea 61 reproduced). Ungated beats every gated arm on B136 at matched gross. 281. re-price-every-ranked-candidate-at-its-Sharpe-maximising-turnover — idea 273 found corr(turnover, Sharpe) of -0.946/-0.986 on broad and -0.668 on u56 across a 0.0028 gross span, which implies every ranked book the record has published at its natural cadence was priced at the wrong end of that curve. Re-run the record's ranked KEEP-candidates and near-misses with the buffer attached and report how many verdicts move, separating cells whose Sharpe gain survives the rule-8 selector from those that do not. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06) — **ANSWERED / KEEP-candidate 4b by-product (2026-09-06, lane C).** The turnover curve is GENERAL: corr(turnover,Sharpe) < 0 in 10/12 published ranked books @10bps (median -0.764) and 11/12 @25bps (-0.942) over a per-book realised-gross span of <= 0.0071, the only exceptions being the 2 MONTHLY books (idea 279's collapse). **4 of 12 published 4b verdicts move @10bps** (3 KILL->KEEP: u56 200d/D, u56 200d/Q, broad 200d/D; 1 KEEP->KILL: u56 none/W gains +0.141 Sharpe and drops through the CAGR floor at 0.87x/yr), 5 of 12 @25bps; **4a stays 0/12, 0/216 cells** — no ranked book, buffered or not, beats RULES v2. The moves **survive the rule-8 selector at the VERDICT level** (rule-8 m == oracle m verdict in 12/12 books at both rungs; plateau, not an island) **but not at the OOS-Sharpe level** (@10bps the rule-8 arm beats its parent OOS in 7/12 and the grid MEAN in only 4/12, median Spearman(IS,OOS) +0.213; @25bps 10/12, 9/12, +0.558) — the gain is a COST effect the 10 bps rung is too small to make selectable. Every mover is a turnover extreme (both DAILY parents at 23.0x/32.2x per yr, plus the quarterly book whose binding axis is DD); the 3 broad WEEKLY H2 near-misses do NOT move. By-product KEEP-candidate 4b: `u56 top20-200d DAILY + m=50` 11.71%/1.1454/-12.37%, halves 1.124/1.169, OOS 13.49%/1.2567, 5.80x/yr, 8/9 cells pass, 4b at 5/10/25 bps (fails 50), KILL 4a — RECORD, do not adopt (min-half 1.124 < live 1.191). No RULES change. `2026-09-06_re-price-every-ranked-candidate-at-its-Sharpe-maximising-turnover_C.py`, memos `.result.md` / `.KEEP_MEMO.md`

(1) **No timing value**: against a CONSTANT gross holding the same realised mean exposure, the budget is worth dSharpe mean -0.0034 (median +0.0004, positive in 97/192) pooled over 2 panels x 2 books x 2 cost rungs, and the sign flips with the book (+0.013 on the live de-grossing book, -0.021 on the ranked one). 273. turnover-budget-the-broad-ranked-book — idea 70 split the broad `top20-200d` H2 Sharpe gap exactly and found selection is +0.1145 while the turnover bill is -0.0979 at 14.37x/yr: the 4b-binding term is the bill, not the alpha. Sweep an explicit turnover budget on that exact book (a no-trade band on the composite rank, e.g. only replace a holding once it falls past rank 20+m) and report Sharpe against turnover, testing whether -0.098 can be halved for less than +0.05 of lost selection. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06) — **ANSWERED (2026-09-06, lane B). The queued test PASSES and in the stronger direction; verdict PARK, no KEEP, no RULES change (4a 0 of 135, 4b 4 of 90 broad).** 3 reproduction gates first: idea 66's broad `top20-200d` 6/6 EXACT (13.11%/0.9581/-20.05%, halves 1.125/0.814), the buffer state machine NESTS the parent at max|weight diff| and max|return diff| both **0.0e+00** (which required carrying the parent's tie quirk — `rank<=20` selects 21 names on 7 of 975 dates), and idea 70's H2 split 4/4 MATCH (SEL +0.1144 / BILL -0.0979 / UND -0.0395 = dS -0.0231, residual 8.3e-17, k 1.2888). **The bill is halved in 30/45 cells and 24/45 do it for <= 0.05 of selection** — but the sign is the real result: slope d(SEL)/d(BILL saved) median **-0.399**, mean -0.949, so removing churn RECOVERS selection rather than costing it. Turnover 13.78x -> 3.46x/yr lifts full Sharpe 0.958 -> **1.061** and MaxDD -20.05% -> **-17.57%**; corr(turnover, Sharpe) **-0.946 @10bps and -0.986 @25bps** over a realised-gross span of 0.0028 (0.7390-0.7418), so this is NOT a gross comparison (idea 274's test). H2 dSharpe vs SPY turns positive in 17/45. **PARK because the 4b passers are islands the selector misses:** the 4 broad passers (m=5/j=2, m=15/j={3,5,inf}, all @10bps, 0/45 @25bps) clear H2 by +0.002 to +0.014 and MaxDD by +0.07 to +0.50pp with **0-2 of 8 neighbours passing** (idea 171's pattern), while the rule-8 IS pick is **m=999/j=1** at both rungs (OOS 11.97%/0.918/-17.57%: +0.024 vs parent, +0.034 vs SPY, **-0.203 vs the live book**, -0.009 vs SPY at 25 bps) and **fails 4b on H2** (0.775 vs 0.837); Spearman(IS,OOS) +0.330/+0.547, IS-best never OOS-best. **The u56 transfer is not confirmation:** 45/45 cells clear 4b there but so does the un-budgeted parent, so the pass belongs to `top20-200d` on u56, not to the budget (though m=999 there is 11.90%/**1.142**/**-12.79%** at 3.49x/yr, OOS 1.210); Spearman(IS,OOS) on u56 is **+0.010**, i.e. the selector is uninformative and the plateau does the work. Caveat restated: 2017-2026 ~= H2, so the OOS bar and the 4b H2 bar overlap (idea 111). `2026-09-06_turnover-budget-the-broad-ranked-book_B.py`, result memo `.result.md`
278. does-v2s-2017-2026-only-Sharpe-edge-have-a-regime-name — idea 274's rule-8 result is that the live book's Sharpe advantage over a constant-gross control does not exist in 2009-2016 and is worth +0.18 OOS. Split the v2-minus-CG(g*) return difference by SPY regime (200d state, vol tercile, drawdown depth) in each window and test whether the IS/OOS gap is a change in the GATE's behaviour or a change in the window's crash content (idea 111/257's denominator question). Max 2 params. (2026-09-06, lane C) — **ANSWERED / KILL of the regime hypothesis (2026-09-06, lane C). No KEEP-candidate, no RULES change (4a 0 of 108 rows, 4b 7 of 108 and none IS-selected).** The gap reproduces exactly against the gross-matched control (u56 @10bps, CG g*=0.55: dSharpe IS -0.0043 -> OOS +0.1492, swing +0.1535). **It is not the window's crash content.** The gate's MECHANICS are identical across windows (realised gross conditional on regime moves <=0.03: above200 0.5824 -> 0.5922, below200 0.2745 -> 0.2564, volHI 0.3952 -> 0.3947), and shift-share puts only **7.7% (state200) / 7.3% (volterciles) / 2.0% (FULL cuts)** of the +0.954 pp/yr mean-d gap on MIX; the ddbucket axis is unusable (MIX 22%, INTERACTION -278%, because SPY's dd20+ bucket is 15.0% of IS days against 2.3% of OOS). Re-weighting OOS days to IS regime composition **does not shrink the Sharpe edge** — +0.1603 state200 (ESS 99.9%), +0.1294 volterciles (13% of the gap), +0.4622 ddbucket (ESS 57.9%, discount) — against an IS value of -0.0043. **What it actually is:** v2 LOSES to the matched control on mean return in BOTH windows (-1.59 -> -0.64 pp/yr); its Sharpe is a pure vol effect (vol ratio 0.8294 -> 0.8275, unchanged), so the swing is 104.4% MEAN channel and 1.7% VOL on u56 — and it REVERSES on broad (MEAN 24.4% / VOL 76.9%, vol ratio 0.8089 -> 0.7719). The channel does not replicate across panels: idea 111 stands, unnamed. Rule 8: the chooser over the 9 regime-conditional arms picks `gate only in above200` and gives up **-0.2550 of OOS Sharpe** vs doing nothing (broad picks CG g=1.00, -0.0179). **PARK by-product:** `gate only in dd10-20` clears 4b on u56 at both rungs (12.14%/1.1605/-19.75%, halves 1.169/1.153, OOS 1.2304, +0.0366 over its own gross-matched CG 0.70) but its IS Sharpe 1.0778 ranks below CG, its DD clears by 0.5pp, and on broad it fails 4b on DD while a different bucket (dd20+, 15.0% IS vs 2.3% OOS coverage) passes instead. `2026-09-06_does-v2s-2017-2026-only-Sharpe-edge-have-a-regime-name_C.py`, result memo `.result.md`

274. is-RULES-v2-only-a-de-grossing-effect — v2 (EWall + 200d +/-3% band, de-gross to cash) posts 1.2056 Sharpe at 0.75 gross with 8.66% CAGR and -12.05% MaxDD. Test whether the gate carries information at all by matching the book's realised average gross with a NO-gate constant-gross EWall book and a random-gate book at the same average exposure; if the matched controls reproduce the Sharpe, v2 is an exposure choice and its CAGR ceiling is structural. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06, lane C) — **ANSWERED / SPLIT (2026-09-06, lane C). No KEEP-candidate, no RULES change (4a 0 of 396 rows).** Reproduction of the live book EXACT (8.66%/1.20562/-12.055%, halves 1.22593/1.19084, turnover 1.774x). **The CAGR ceiling IS structural:** the un-gated constant-gross ladder's Sharpe is FLAT — 1.1221 -> 1.1245 over gross 0.20 -> 1.00 (span **0.0023**) while CAGR runs 3.50% -> 17.74% — so gross is a pure CAGR/MaxDD scaler and v2's 8.66% is an exposure choice; at matched realised gross (0.5328 vs nominal 0.75) the gate is CAGR-**negative**, -1.04 pp/yr on u56 and -2.32 pp/yr on broad. **Sharpe: not de-grossing on u56, but not in-sample either.** u56 @10bps v2 1.2056 vs CG(g*=0.55) 1.1235 (+0.0821, and above every rung), 96.7th pct of the rotated-gate null RGT (2/60) and 95.0th of the label-permuted null RGP (3/60), breakeven 56.5 bps — both nulls turnover-matched by construction (1.774/1.795 vs 1.774), so this is an information test not idea 262's turnover test. **But rule 8 rejects it:** on 2009-2016 alone v2 is **1.1043 against CG's 1.1116**, so the IS chooser picks CG g=1.00 (OOS 1.1353/-29.2%) over the live book (OOS 1.2851/-12.1%); v2's whole Sharpe edge is a 2017-2026 window statistic (idea 111), selection-loses instance #11. **On broad it IS only a de-grossing effect, and a losing one:** Sharpe 1.1058 at the 40th pct of RGT (12/20 beat it) and the **0th of RGP (20/20 beat it)**, CAGR 0th pct of RGT (z -4.17), CG(g*) beats it on Sharpe AND CAGR, breakeven 2.30 bps. **What survives everywhere: drawdown.** v2's MaxDD is the best of EVERY draw of both nulls on both panels at both rungs (0/60 and 0/20 shallower; z +1.90/+2.85 u56, +5.36/+3.81 broad), -12.05% vs CG(g*)'s -16.90% at the same average exposure — v2 is a drawdown instrument, not a return instrument. 4b 3 of 396, all un-gated CG rungs on u56 (g=0.65 @10bps 11.48%/1.1238/-19.75%, halves 1.1895/1.0720, OOS 1.1358) — **PARK, a one-rung knife edge** (g=0.60 fails CAGR by 0.07pp, g=0.70 fails DD by 0.9pp, window 0.05 of gross wide, inside idea 154's +/-0.03 band, and neither walk-forward selector lands on it). `2026-09-06_is-RULES-v2-only-a-de-grossing-effect_C.py`, result memo `.result.md`
70. what-actually-moves-H2 — idea 66 leaves the broad ranked book's H2 shortfall (0.814 vs SPY 0.837) unexplained: gross does nothing (0.001) and the SPY core does +0.047 at t<1. Decompose broad H2 excess return by sector and by top-10 mega-cap weight; is it a handful of names or a regime? (2026-09-04, lane B) — **DONE 2026-09-06 (cloud). ANSWERED / KILL of both queued mechanisms.** Not a handful of names: 0 of 128 single-name deletions (banned from eligibility over the whole sample, book reconstitutes) lift H2 above SPY's 0.834; best is USO +0.0291 -> 0.8316, missing by 0.0024; mean dH2 +0.0002, sd 0.0101, corr(contrib,dH2) -0.531. Not mega-cap weight: the uncapped book holds only 0.145 in the PIT top-10 during H2 and capping it moves H2 the WRONG way monotonically (0.8025 -> 0.7372), clearing the bar at 0 of 5 levels; rule 8 picks do-nothing in 3/4 panels (+0.0031 OOS Sharpe). It is a REGIME: inside H2 the book beats SPY by +6.05pp/yr while SPY is below its 200d MA (401d) and loses 7.31pp/yr while SPY is in a <5% drawdown (1474d); it wins 4 of 10 H2 years, all stress or chop (2018 +6.4, 2022 +8.4) and loses the melt-ups (2019 -14.7, 2023 -14.1, 2021 -13.1). Sector proxy: SMH 36.3% of gross H2 attribution, top 3 blocks 67.3%. 4a 0/20, 4b 2/20 (both the standing U56 candidate unchanged). No KEEP-candidate, no RULES change; H2 Sharpe is a WINDOW statistic (see idea 111).**
70. what-actually-moves-H2 (lane B) — **INDEPENDENT SECOND RUN, raced with the cloud entry above and reaching the SAME verdict on both queued mechanisms from a different construction; it adds the exact cost/selection split and localises the residual to 21 days. ANSWERED / KILL of both candidate explanations (2026-09-06, lane B).** The broad `top20-200d` H2 shortfall (0.814 vs SPY 0.837) is neither a handful of names nor a sector hole: it is a BILL and an EPISODE. Reproduction 6/6 EXACT of idea 66's published arm first. Because the failing bar is scale-free, the run splits the VOL-MATCHED active return `a_t = k*r_book - r_spy` (k = 1.2888), for which `sum_i A_i + COST + BENCH = dS` holds to **1.1e-16**: **selection +0.1145, turnover bill -0.0979 (14.37x/yr at 10 bps), vol-matched underinvestment -0.0395 = -0.0231**. The stock picking BEATS SPY by +0.114 and cost eats 85% of it; idea 63's cash drag is worth only -0.040 once vol is matched, exactly as idea 66's flat-Sharpe result implied. **No sector is a hole** (3 of 15 negative, -0.017 combined; InfoTech +0.487 of +0.911), and **MEGA10 is the book's best asset, not its hole** — +0.224 of Sharpe from 13.5% of weight, regression of a_t on MEGA10 weight R^2 0.0012 t +1.66 with a POSITIVE sign, and banning all ten costs -0.084 H2 Sharpe. **136 real leave-one-out re-runs**: only 3 of 136 single-name removals clear SPY (spread 0.780-0.844, sd 0.0105); the hindsight drop-10 ceiling is 0.920 at the 100th percentile of 2000 random 10-subsets. **The residual after cost is ONE 21-day window**, 2020-03-24 -> 2020-04-22 (the 200d gate's post-crash re-entry lag, i.e. ideas 52/57's whipsaw): worth 617% of the gap, deleting it flips dSharpe -0.023 -> **+0.120**, permutation percentile 1.0% at L=21 but 31.5% at L=126 — an episode, not a multi-year regime. Rule 8 (exclusion fitted on 2009-2016, 2017-2026 untouched; H2 IS the OOS window, 100% overlap): 12 grid points, **4a 0/12 against the LIVE book (RULES v2, adopted the same day: 8.04%/1.107/-12.24%, halves 1.229/0.988, OOS 1.122) and 4b 0/12** — against the retired v1 it was 9/12, never informative at OOS 0.581; the name ladder moves OOS Sharpe 0.887-0.912 around the parent's 0.894, and **persistence is the direct KILL — Spearman(IS, OOS) per-name +0.285 with IS-worst-10 vs OOS-worst-10 overlap 0 of 10**. The one point beating SPY OOS (sector k=10, 1.005 vs 0.884) bans 10 of 15 classes, fails 4b on DD (-22.2% vs the 20.2% bar) and is a non-monotone ladder outlier. No KEEP-candidate, no memo, no RULES change. `2026-09-06_what-actually-moves-H2_B.py`

268. price-the-four-unpriced-null-verdicts-on-their-own-books — **ANSWERED / SPLIT 2-2 (2026-09-06, lane B).** The premise is half wrong: all four scripts already ran at 10 AND 25 bps, so "unpriced" can only mean none reported where its breakeven sits. On each script's OWN panel/n/cadence, with every parent construction imported and reproduced exactly (idea 209 corpus 384/384 @2.2e-15, idea 158 grid 546/546 @7.1e-15, idea 181 grid 900 rows @6.7e-16, and idea 192's published pooling rebuilt to **1 of 51 / 31.6% of 237 / Fisher p 9.23e-07** vs its published 1/51, 31.6%, 9.2e-07): **SURVIVES** idea 209's LARGE half (24/24 positive at every rung 0-25, zero breakevens) and idea 192's clause KILL (no breakeven in [0,25]; p 3.41e-02 @0bps -> 2.35e-05 @10 -> 7.05e-06 @15). **FRAGILE** idea 209's SMALL half - the sign reversal its whole verdict rests on is a 14-18 bps phenomenon, mean rho +0.600 @0bps -> +0.395 @10 -> -0.002 @25, keys negative 0/6 -> 1/6 -> 4/6, with 4 of 6 arm-cells breaking inside (0,25] and the RND null itself at 18.12 bps - and idea 158's 27-of-28 count (13/14 @0bps, 14/14 @15+, one cell breaking at 13.99 bps; the delete-the-scaler DIRECTION is intact and the gap widens monotonically +0.088 -> +0.192). **HEADLINE (idea 180): at 0 bps the five real keys sit at the 55th / 50th / 45th percentile of their own 100-draw null band - inside it, and BELOW the null mean, on all three panels.** The published 92nd/100th/51st is bought entirely by the rung, because the random-walk key is the LEAST disruptive tilt (turnover ratio to the control 1.05-1.10 vs 1.12-1.29 for every real key), so **a matched-null |dSharpe| band read at a non-zero rung is a turnover test, not an information test**. Incidental: idea 158's zero-information-book-clears-4b is a 10-bps statement (RAND 4b passes 19 @0bps / 11 @10 / 0 @20; all 4b passes 0 @25). Idea 262's CLASS prices (RWK 11.36, REDRAWN 0.99 bps) do not transfer - the actual sentence breakevens run 4.11-20.20 bps; price the sentence, not the class. Rule 8: IS-argmax over 465 books picks S1/broad/R6/n=3 -> OOS 17.8%/0.820/-30.2% vs SPY OOS 0.882/15.45%/-33.72% = KILL 4b (OOS, DD) at every rung, and the SELECTION rung changes the book (2 picks, OOS Sharpe spread 0.134). 4,464 grid rows / 5,118 books; 4a 65 and 4b 54 of 273 at 10 bps, all already-known rows; **no KEEP-candidate, no new book, no RULES change**. `2026-09-06_price-the-four-unpriced-null-verdicts-on-their-own-books_B.py`

269. is-the-reversal-share-a-function-of-n-over-n_elig — **ANSWERED / KILL of the premise (2026-09-06, lane C).** Reversal share is NOT monotone in `n/n_elig`: on a matched-ratio grid (5 panels x 7 ratios, n = round(r*n_elig), idea 259's construction, reproduction 2/2 EXACT) the share runs 0.60/0.60/**0.80/0.80**/0.40/0.40/0.00 over r* = 0.05..1.00 — it RISES before it falls — and the pooled Spearman -0.386 (t -2.40) collapses to **-0.181 (t -0.97)** once the r=1 IDENTITY cell (where FWD-n *is* EWall) is dropped. The ratio is not the variable: at MATCHED r the five panels disagree **0.00 vs 1.00 in 6 of 7 rows** (B136 6/7, BSTK100 6/7, U56 4/7, ETF36 2/7, **SMALL439 0/7** at every ratio and both rungs), and Spearman(rev,n) -0.317 / (rev,n_elig) -0.081 are no better. The census cannot arbitrate — with 35,875 of 37,044 ranked pairs back-filled (96.8%, 84 files) its sign is a WEIGHTING choice: pair-level **+0.351**, point-level **-0.342**, file-clustered **+0.590**, because r there varies almost entirely between panels. What survives is one-sided and weak: an IS-fitted `r >= 0.55` screen classifies OOS reversals at **0.743 vs a 0.543 base rate** (rivals CONST 0.543, per-panel majority 0.657). Rule 8 book: RSEL picks a different n on 5/5 panels and gains nothing — pooled OOS 9.6%/**0.897**/-21.4% against FWD20 11.1%/0.903/-21.4%, EWALL 8.6%/0.857, RULES v1 6.4%/0.691, SPY 15.5%/0.882. 4b 8/40 at 10 bps, every passer a known point on idea 259's own two ladders; SMALL439/ETF36 0 of 16. 269. back-fill-the-breakeven-column-arithmetically-not-by-re-running — **ANSWERED / SPLIT (2026-09-06, lane B).** The arithmetic needs THREE committed numbers, not four: vol is recoverable from (CAGR, Sharpe) via `vol^2/2 - Sharpe*vol + ln(1+CAGR) = 0` (positive root) at **3.96e-4 median relative error, 98.6% within 1% on 10061 committed triples**, so the record's Vol column is redundant. The law itself is not a null-arm artefact: on the record's NON-NULL dials (150 books, 3675 pairs, turnover ratios 0.06x-34x) measured-vs-predicted breakeven is **R^2 0.9990 IS / 0.9994 OOS**, median error 0.015/0.017 bps. But the BACK-FILL's REACH is the binding constraint: only **315 of 2238 DISTINCT leaderboard rows (14.1%)** can be priced, because the leaderboard commits no turnover and 74% of rows have a parent that commits none either (tier D 847 + tier E 1656 of 3753). Headline: **13 of 305 distinct non-degenerate back-filled rows (4.3%)** have a breakeven inside (0,25] bps — all of them published KILL 4b verdicts that SOFTEN with cost (9 share one parent, `2026-09-04_sector-only-universe_C.py`), none a KEEP a higher rung would overturn. Incidental: **LEADERBOARD.md carries 1515 byte-identical duplicate rows**, so any count quoted off it without de-duplication is inflated. Rule 8: IS-argmax `B136/FWD10/W` -> OOS 12.8%/0.781/-21.4% vs SPY OOS 0.882 — KILL 4b (H2,OOS,DD). 900 grid points, 14 pass 4b at 10 bps and every one is a book the record already holds; **no new KEEP-candidate, no RULES change**.
259. does-the-sharpe-cagr-reversal-sit-under-every-EWall-claim (2026-09-06, lane C) — **ANSWERED; the reversal is GENERAL. KILL of Sharpe-only reporting for un-ranked-vs-ranked comparisons.** 771 committed CSVs scanned (.csv and .csv.gz), 97 carry an EWall arm beside another with both metrics: **44,358 pairs, 37,044 RANKED across 91 files**. EWall wins on Sharpe 25,878 but on CAGR only 13,317; **reversals 12,475 = 33.7%**, **12,280 (98.4%) in the queue's direction**, and **conditional on EWall winning on Sharpe it LOSES on CAGR in 47.5%**; 87 of 91 files have >=1 reversal, 18 of 91 a majority; OOS 30.6% on 35,343 pairs. Against NON-ranked comparands the rate is only 25.0%, so it is specific to un-ranked-vs-RANKED. Controlled re-read (idea 82's construction verbatim, 4 panels x 6 n): reproduction EXACT (`B136/EWall` 10.7%/1.026/-17.7% OOS 1.019; `U56/EWall` fails 4b on CAGR alone; gate `EWall-FWD` Sharpe +0.0410 t +3.52 7/9 vs published +0.0467/+4.03, `FWD-EWall` CAGR +1.26pp t +5.49 9/9 vs +1.28/+4.93), and on all 21 unsaturated cells the two headlines SEPARATE: **Sharpe +0.0359 t +1.79 14/21 (panel-fragile) vs CAGR -2.26 pp/yr t -6.65 0/21 (unanimous)**. **Not cost:** reversals 13/21 at 0 bps vs 14/21 at 10, gap 3.02pp before costs and 2.26 after. A pre-registered vol-ratio band predicts reversals with **zero false negatives** on both corpora (grid accuracy 0.896, census recall 1.000/precision 0.501) — a NECESSARY condition, usable as a screen. **Rule 8:** S_SHARPE and S_CAGR pick a DIFFERENT arm in **3 of 4 panels**, worth **+2.53 pp/yr OOS CAGR for -0.0254 OOS Sharpe**; OOS Sharpe EWALL 0.8577 > FWD20 0.8428 > S_SHARPE 0.7815 > S_CAGR 0.7561, all below SPY 0.882 (another idea-229 selection-loses instance). KEEP paths 4a 5/32 / 4b 6/32 at 10 bps, all already-known rows; SMALL439 0 of 16. Reporting habit proposed (publish CAGR + realised vol beside Sharpe on any un-ranked-vs-ranked comparison); no RULES change, no book promoted. research/backtests/2026-09-06_does-the-sharpe-cagr-reversal-sit-under-every-EWall-claim_C.result.md . 262. is-the-1bp-breakeven-general-to-the-records-null-arms — **ANSWERED / KILL of the premise, twice (2026-09-06, lane C).** All 3636 points reported. (i) The record does NOT build every null held-or-re-drawn: of 194 scripts, 59 call an RNG and only **9 build a null that TRADES** (HELD 4, REDRAWN 2, NOISE 3); all 11 PERM and 33 SUBSET_PANEL sites were hand-read and **0 of 11 PERM is a time-varying pick**. (ii) The 1-bp breakeven is a monotone function of the **turnover ratio**, not a property of nulls: median vs FWD RANDW **0.99** (3.66x) / ROTW 0.75 (3.81x) / RANDM 6.45 (1.45x) / RWK **11.36** (1.22x) / RANDH **16.89** (0.79x, never flips in 50/72 draws). `ROTW` (same churn, zero randomness) prices identically, so it is the churn, not the draw. **4 of 9 scripts carry an unpriced null at or below 10 bps**, three of them the random-walk shape the dichotomy does not name; `RWK - FWD` reverses sign inside the record's own rung range (+0.0217 t+3.86 at 0 bps, -0.0156 t-2.73 at 10). The law `c* = dSharpe(0)*1e4/(T_x/vol_x - T_y/vol_y)` reproduces the measured breakeven at **R^2 0.9989, median error 0.028 bps** over 337 points, so idea 263's column is provably sufficient and needs no re-runs. No KEEP, no memo, no RULES change. `2026-09-06_is-the-1bp-breakeven-general-to-the-records-null-arms_C.py`

The relationship instead dies on SMALL484 — the worst-survivorship panel — where the raw slope is +0.11 (t 0.35, R2 0.0008) while winner content alone has t +5.00, i.e. the opposite of the thermometer prediction; idea 73's level result is a large-cap phenomenon. As a rule-8 panel selector, IS dispersion beats a random panel 6/8 (+0.023 OOS Sharpe) but inverts on SMALL484 and loses to holding the whole panel (0.989 vs 1.020) — KILL as a selector.

83. dispersion-as-a-survivorship-detector — **ANSWERED, hypothesis REFUTED; KILL of the thermometer reading (2026-09-06, lane B).** The premise HOLDS (Spearman(sd, W) +0.473, Pearson +0.567; W alone R2 0.155, t +5.22) but the inference does NOT: with each name's own full-sample return regressed out, dispersion keeps **62%** of its univariate R2 (0.306 → **0.190**, t **+5.87**) against a pre-registered bar of kill<1/3 and |t|<2, surviving |t|>2 in **3/3** count-matched k-cells; adding the draw's best name as a 2nd control makes sd STRONGER (+6.92), and the conditioning reverses — W's own t falls +5.22 → +1.43 once sd is in. Rule 8 agrees: the residualised selector S5 keeps 22% of S2's edge (regret +0.064 vs +0.292, z −0.31) and the pure-winnerness selector S6 is the only one that LOSES to doing nothing (−0.117). Idea 78's test C is clean on this axis; idea 54's panel-level concern is untouched. Reproduction 300/300 rows at ≤7.1e-15. 4a 276/450, 4b 87/450, nothing promoted. `2026-09-06_dispersion-as-a-survivorship-detector_B.py`

240. is-n20-a-constant-or-a-grid-edge — DONE 2026-09-06 (lane C). **KILL of 'take the widest n', and a CORRECTION to idea 77's 7-of-7 claim: n=20 was a GRID EDGE.** Idea 73/77's panels, gate, key, gross, cadence and cost imported verbatim; idea 77's published n=20 IS/OOS column reproduces on all 7 panels to **0.0004**. 98 points (7 panels x n in {5,10,20,30,40,60} x 2 weighting conventions + 14 references), all reported; two tuned parameters (panel, n). **(1)** On the widened grid {20,30,40,60} the OOS argmax splits **3 at n=20 / 1 interior / 3 at n=60** under BOTH conventions — 4 of 7 panels move the moment 30/40/60 exist, so idea 77's 'n=20 in 7 of 7' was the top of idea 73's grid, not a constant. **(2) But 'widest' is dead too:** pooled WIDEST60-WIDEST20 = +0.0609 (FIXED) / +0.0335 (NORM) on only **4/7 and 3/7 panels**, with the RANDOM control (0.994) sitting BETWEEN the two edges. **(3) What the dial actually is:** **Spearman(mean eligible count, OOS argmax n) = +0.926 on both conventions** (N=7, descriptive) — the 3 panels that stay at 20 are exactly the 3 that cannot fill more (STK20 under-fills n=20 in 99.3% of weeks, ETF24 47.6%, ETF36 20.6%) and the 3 that reach 60 are the 3 largest, where 60 is AGAIN the grid top; the ceiling above 60 eligible names is still unlocated. **(4) Half the premium is the gross ladder:** idea 73's fixed GROSS/n weight silently de-grosses a wide book (U56 n=60 holds mean gross 0.474 vs 0.721 at n=20). Holding gross at 0.75 halves the pooled effect, drops it to 3/7 panels, REVERSES U56 n=30 vs n=20 (1.102 vs 1.092 -> 1.055 vs 1.068) and turns BSTK100/n=60's MaxDD from -17.1% into -26.4% — idea 135's control, confirmed. **(5) Rule 8:** doing NOTHING (incumbent U56/n=20) 1.168 beats WIDEST60 1.030, RANDOM 0.994, ISARGMAX 0.976 and WIDEST20 0.969 — a **12th instance for idea 229's pool**, and the IS reading of the dial (+0.0070) is worse than a coin flip (+0.0244). **KEEP paths:** 4a 23/42 FIXED and 4/42 NORM; 4b 11/42 and 13/42. Best new row BSTK100/n=60 (FIXED) 11.4%/1.108/-17.1%, halves 1.178/1.040, OOS 1.129, 6.3x/yr, 4a+4b — **not promoted**: it fails 4b under NORM on the drawdown bar, so the entire margin is silent de-grossing, on a 100-name current-constituent list. RULES wording recommended: none. RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py, baseline.py untouched. Memo: research/backtests/2026-09-06_is-n20-a-constant-or-a-grid-edge_C.result.md . Ideas 242-244 queued.
77. what-predicts-a-panel — **KILL for a universe clause; the +0.710 is a PANEL effect that carries NO ranking information** (2026-09-06, lane B). Idea 73's construction imported unchanged; its two published Spearmans reproduce **exactly** (+0.710 and +0.134) and its harness rows to the last digit before anything new was read. 35 points (7 panels x 5 books), 9 predictors, all reported; two tuned parameters (panel, n) — the predictors are measurements, no book reads one. **(1) It is the panel, not the n dial:** between-panel Spearman(mean IS, mean OOS) = **+0.857** (exact perm p 0.0238 over all 5,040 orderings of 7 panels) while the IS argmax n matches the OOS argmax n in only **2 of 7** panels (OOS wants n=20 in 7/7, which IS never picks). **(2) It carries no ranking information:** the panel's own **un-ranked** equal-weight-all-eligible book (`EWall_S`) predicts the ranked book's OOS Sharpe BETTER than the ranked book's own IS Sharpe does — +0.857 (p **0.024**) vs +0.821 (p 0.034), and +0.712 vs +0.710 on the 21 points. **(3) Of the three candidates the queue named, LEVEL wins:** level_S +0.750 (p 0.066); persistence ts_mom +0.464 (p 0.302) / xs_mom +0.107 (p 0.840); **correlation is dead** (-0.071 at n=20, negative or nil under every partial control, -0.591..+0.114). level_S collapses to -0.155 controlling for the ranked IS Sharpe, i.e. they are the same fact. ts_mom is NEGATIVE on all 7 panels (12-1 momentum is a per-name reversal signal everywhere here). **(4) Rule 8:** S_LVL and S_TSMOM both pick STK20/CAND20 (OOS 13.9%/**1.446**/-12.1%, 4b PASS, +0.477 over the 0.969 coin flip) — one lucky panel with two labels; **S_CORR and S_DISP land on the SAME wrong panel** (SMALL484, 0.510, -0.459); and **doing NOTHING (U56/CAND20, 1.168, +0.199) beats the IS-Sharpe chooser read at n=20 (BSTK100, 0.938, -0.031) on an IS margin of 0.013** — an 11th instance for idea 229's pool. **Nothing promoted:** the winning predictor is LEVEL, and level on current-constituent lists is what survivorship fabricates, so 'run the book where the names went up in-sample' is unfalsifiable on this data. RULES wording recommended: none; PROTOCOL reporting habit proposed instead — publish the panel's un-ranked `EWall` Sharpe beside any panel-choice claim, since it carries the whole predictable part. Memo: research/backtests/2026-09-06_what-predicts-a-panel_B.result.md. Ideas 239-241 queued.

221. is-phase-sensitivity-a-book-property-or-a-panel-one (2026-09-06, cloud) — **KILL, and it is NEITHER: phase is a panel-DATE shock, not a book trait.** 115 books x 6 cadences x their phases = 3680 runs; controls PASS ([c] reproduces idea 187's phase.csv on all 2300 rows at 2.220e-16, [d] reproduces idea 76's episodes.csv on all 805 rows at 5.684e-14, Q k=1 negative control exactly 0.000e+00). **(1)** Per-book 6W spread is 0.3787 ALL / 0.3459 SMALL / 0.3979 U56 / 0.4084 ETF, and a book's spread does not reproduce at another block length — cross-cadence rank stability median rho **+0.086** over 10 cadence pairs; family dummies explain only **0.066** of log-spread variance. **(2)** The idea's mechanism is **reversed in sign within every family**: P1 (episode length / block) gives within-family rho **SMALL +0.130 / U56 +0.540 / ETF +0.177** (U56 8W rho +0.732, t +5.98) against a pre-registered NEGATIVE; P2 (eligible-name turnover) is flat (+0.034 pooled median). The strongest correlate is the control n_names (rho -0.36..-0.40) — small books have big spreads, a diversification effect. **(3)** The IS-only screen FAILS: P1 holds in 1/5 cadences and fails in 2 (at 6W the predicted-insensitive half is WORSE, 0.4035 vs 0.3536, t -2.80). **(4) Post-hoc mechanism (labelled):** argmax-phase agreement across books is **0.73 median vs 0.17 chance** (0.97 at 6W/U56, 1.00 at 2M/U56) and the common share of phase variance is **0.675 median (U56 0.750, ETF 0.835, SMALL 0.279)** — on large caps 62-94% of the phase effect is ONE profile every book eats on the same dates, so there is no idiosyncratic residual to predict. **(5) Rule 8:** SCREENED keeps only **+0.0151 (t 1.80)** of REC's **+0.0388 (t 3.26)** over the weekly incumbent, while **PHASE-AVG wins at +0.0425 (t 3.53)** with no book property at all. KEEP paths on 3680 rows: 4a 404, 4b 137 (all U56), but only **3 of 86 (book,cadence) cells pass 4b at all of their own phases** — no candidate. Recommendation to Sunday review: do NOT soften idea 187's clause 12 into a per-book screen; the implementable answer is idea 222's phase-averaged book. RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py, baseline.py untouched.
183. the-anchor-position-column (2026-09-06, lane B) — **KILL as a trading rule; the schema column is DELIVERED, and idea 183's own premise survives only in a WEAKER, TWO-SIDED form.** Back-fill over 570 committed CSVs -> 29 files / **10,593 auditable ladders** in 6 dials, with control [b] reproducing idea 173's committed .anchorposition.csv on all 90 rows at **2.6e-16** and re-deriving its published 0.74 / 0.32 exactly. THE COUNT ASKED FOR: **1,951 of 10,593 (18.4%) auditable ladders had the control at a grid edge; 1,248 of 2,509 (49.7%) on the five headline dials; 1,067 of 1,656 (64.4%) of the ladders where the claim was actually made** — driven by CADENCE, **79.4% of whose ladders START at W**, so RULES v1's own cadence has no faster neighbour and every 'slower beats weekly' sentence in the record is measured from the low edge of its own grid. The pooled 3.50x over-representation is a **Simpson artefact** of COST (7,402 ladders, 0.3% edge share) mixing with BAND/SLEEVE (100% edge by construction); stratified it is 0.81x and is NOT claimed. Re-anchoring sweep (51,764 rows): moving ONLY the control on byte-identical ladders swings 'a random draw beats the control' from **0.166 at the low grid edge to 0.211 at the middle to 0.864 at the high edge (spread 0.698)** — the outcome-RANK version idea 173 implied is a tautology (0.000 at rank 1, 1.000 at rank K) and is not scored. **P4 MISSED in the informative direction:** median claimed margin at an edge is **0.58x** the middle, not >1.5x, because the record's edge anchors sit at the LOW end of increasing dials where the control is often the ladder's BEST point — the bias is two-sided and unsignable without the column. Rule 8 on 9 fresh ladders (75 backtests, params <=2016-12-31, 2017-2026 read once): ANCHOR 0.6667 / IS-PICK 0.7025 / ORACLE 0.8151 vs RULES v1 0.6827 and SPY 0.8820; the interior-anchor filter removes 0 of 9 (all interior) so its OOS delta is exactly 0.0000 and IS-PICK minus ANCHOR is +0.0359, inside the pre-registered no-content band — the column is a discount on claims, not a selector of books. 4a 7/75, 4b 3/75 (U56 COUNT n=15/20/30; n=20 is also the rule-8 IS pick, 12.86%/1.1075/-18.21%, OOS 1.1775) — **PARKed and memo'd, NOT proposed**: it fails the universe change (ETF36 H1 0.749, SMALL439 0.487) and is idea 182's already-PARKed U56 top-20 book under idea 144. RULES v1's own n=5 ranks **7/9, 8/9, 8/9** on its own COUNT ladder. Schema in .schema.md: ANCHOR-POS, ANCHOR-EDGE, ANCHOR-RANK. Nothing in RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py or baseline.py touched.
222. a-phase-averaged-cadence-as-the-honest-estimator — idea 187 shows a single-phase ladder point is an alignment draw, not a cadence estimate. Build the phase-averaged book (equal-weight blend of all k phases at cadence k, or the mean of their returns) and re-run idea 175's ladder on it: does a phase-averaged ladder have the humped shape idea 175 claimed, and does the M-vs-6W split of idea 188 survive averaging? This is the estimator every future cadence claim should use. Max 2 params. (2026-09-06, lane B) (2026-09-06, lane C) — **ANSWERED: KILL of BOTH published shape claims. Idea 175's humped ladder and idea 188's M-vs-6W family split are BLOCK-PHASE artefacts and neither survives averaging.** 6785 runs = idea 175/187's 115 books x 13 points x all k phases; reproduction 5/5 before any new number ([c] PH0 ladder == idea 175's 805/805 at 7.105e-15, 0 verdict mismatches; [d] phase sweep == idea 187's 1955/1955 at 2.220e-16, its 345 surplus Q rows degenerate at 0.000e+00; [e] MEANPH/BLEND-DR bitwise 0.0 at every k=1 point). Four estimators reported: PH0 (the record's convention), MEANPH (mean across phases — the de-confounded shape estimator), BLEND-DR / BLEND-BH (the queue's two blends). **(1) The ladder stops being jagged and the argmax moves to M in every family:** slow-zone {6W..10W} spread 0.2198 -> 0.0491 pooled (4.5x), U56 0.3108 -> 0.0637, ETF 0.2859 -> 0.0570; idea 187's 6W->7W collapse of -0.174 becomes -0.003; idea 175's 6W-minus-W +0.0999 ALL / +0.1640 U56 / +0.1600 ETF becomes **-0.0029 / -0.0500 / -0.0430**, its monotone rise D->6W is False, and the 6W..10W zone share falls 80.0% -> 7.8%. What survives runs between M and Q, both k=1 points with no phase freedom — real cadence content, none of it at 6W. Q stays the worst of 13 under every estimator (P4 missed: Q's badness is not phase). **(2) Idea 188's split INVERTS rather than fades:** M-minus-6W goes U56 -0.1171 (t -13.99, 0W/33L) -> **+0.0967 (t +13.03, 33W/0L)** and ETF -0.0870 (t -16.36, 0W/33L) -> **+0.1163 (t +13.81, 32W/1L)**; SMALL's +0.0815 -> +0.0419 survives and is the weakest of the three. The signal-horizon mechanism explained a sign that exists only at 6W phase 0. **(3) The BLEND confound, named and measured:** BLEND-DR minus MEANPH is exactly 0.0000 at every k=1 point and rises with sleeve count to +0.0333 at k=16, Spearman(k, bonus) +0.8883 — a blended book tilts slow for a diversification reason; too small to move the argmax here (P6 partial). **(4) Rule 8, fourteenth straight do-nothing win but a 6.4x cheaper one:** SEL-SHARPE minus best constant -0.1584 (t -11.76) at PH0 vs -0.0246 (t -2.93) at MEANPH; CONST-M (0.7559) is the only arm identical under all four estimators. SPY OOS 15.45%/0.8820/-33.72%; RULES v1 OOS 0.7471 (U56) / 0.4923 (SMALL). **(5) KEEP paths on 5980 rows: 4a/4b PH0 272/90 (reproduces idea 187), MEANPH 232/73, BLEND 254/90; SMALL 0 and ETF 0 on 4b at every estimator = fourteenth reproduction of idea 136. U56 @ 2M holds its published 4b pass ONLY at phase 0 and loses it under averaging (U56 @ 2W gains one); 37 of 1495 cells flip.** 4 of 8 predictions hit, P2 in substance, P3 in all three families but not pooled, P4 and P6-second-half missed. Proposed for Sunday review: PROTOCOL clause 12 (phase-averaged cadence + publish the blend bonus). No new book, no KEEP; RULES.md, scan.py, bot.py, baseline.py untouched. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-06_a-phase-averaged-cadence-as-the-honest-estimator_C.py`; `.result.md` alongside.

leg (b) DVOL +0.5777 small (6/12); **leg (c) MCAP NOT RUN — needs shares outstanding, no series cached and no internet; stays idea 195**. **(2) The leak-free analogue kills the economics:** DDTR/NEG (trailing 252d drawdown, knowable at t — the implementable 'buy the beaten-down') is **+0.0519 broad / -0.0653 small and clears the null band positively 0 of 24 cells**. **(3) The tilt is priced by its leak:** Spearman(|IC vs realised forward total return|, mean NEG dSharpe) = **+0.806 broad / +0.881 small** over 10/12 keys; oracle-conditioned, declared) — PRICE/NEG's dSharpe is **deep-minus-shallow -0.1203, 0/6 cells positive** (at m=1.0/10bps +0.1525 deep vs +0.3643 shallow); DDTR -0.1674, DVOL -0.2411, FROZEN -0.0440. **(5) Rule 8 (4 cells): S1 over ALL keys beats do-nothing by +1.1744 (t +3.89) picking FWDRET/POS in 3 of 4 cells and PRICE/NEG in the fourth; 187. is-6W-a-grid-edge-or-a-real-optimum (2026-09-06, lane B) — **KILL, and the idea's dichotomy is FALSE on both horns: cadence LENGTH is not the variable, block PHASE is.** 13-point ladder D/2D/W/2W/M/6W/7W/8W/2M/10W/Q/16W/2Q on idea 175's 115 books verbatim, 1495 runs; the 7 shared points reproduce idea 175's committed ladder.csv on all **805 rows at 7.105e-15** with 0 verdict mismatches, so the new points are comparable bar for bar. **(1) The argmax MOVES (6W -> 2M pooled) and is INTERIOR in 4/4 family rows, so truncation is refuted (0 of 7 slower-than-6W points beat 6W on ALL/U56/ETF) — but the ladder is JAGGED, not humped**: 7W 0.606 sits below every point from W on, 2M (6.1 reb/yr) beats 8W (6.5 reb/yr) by +0.175, **Q (4.0/yr) is the WORST point on the whole ladder while 2Q at half its frequency beats it**, and 6W-minus-7W on U56 is **-0.3108 (t -29.65)**, 1.9x the entire 6W-minus-W gap idea 175 published. **(2) The labelled post-hoc phase control names the mechanism**: holding cadence FIXED and sweeping the block phase gives spreads of **0.1518 (t 5.41) ALL / 0.2618 SMALL / 0.3957 (t 34.12) U56 / 0.3862 (t 34.89) ETF at 6W alone** — **2.1-2.4x the published 6W-minus-W cadence effect, 16.1x on SMALL** — while **Q returns exactly 0.0000 on all four families as the negative control** (a k=1 calendar point has one phase by construction). The ladder's 6W is **phase 0 of 6 and ranks 1/6 on ALL, U56 and ETF**, and **beats the weekly incumbent at only 2 of its own 6 phases**; a phase is fixed by an arbitrary sample-start date and is not a tradable choice, so idea 175's headline is an alignment draw. **(3) Rule 8 kills the selector**: SEL-SHARPE goes to **-0.0124 (t -0.68) pooled, capture -5.6%, BEHIND the RANDOM control**, and loses outright on U56 (-0.1377, t -6.57, sign p 0.0046); SEL==ORACLE agreement 5.2% pooled and **0.0% on U56**. Idea 175's +0.0388 (t +3.26) was a 4-point-grid artefact and **idea 107's evidence base is gone**. **(4) KEEP paths on all 1495 rows: 4a 272, 4b 90, every 4b pass on U56** (SMALL 0/637, ETF 0/429); fixed-panel 4b is U56 @ 2M/M/W/2D, the same book re-cadenced (idea 144) — **U56 @ 2M is NOT a candidate** (1-of-13 grid pick, phase 0 of 2, loses to W at its other phase). 188. why-does-the-small-panel-want-M-and-the-large-caps-6W — ANSWERED 2026-09-05 (cloud): SIGNAL HORIZON, not cost. The cost effect (10bps minus 0bps) is -0.0044..-0.0087 of Sharpe = 6-8% of the SMALL gap and pushes EVERY family the same way, so the whole split is already present at ZERO cost (SMALL M-6W +0.1107 t +7.78 full / +0.0902 t +4.19 OOS; U56 -0.1106 t -13.33 with 0W/33L; ETF -0.0807 t -15.28 0W/33L; OOS sign split 9/9 rung cells). The mechanism is the decay curve: eligible-name rank IC on SMALL439 peaks at h=10 and is DEAD by h=30 (IC(30)/IC(21) = 0.241 full, -0.168 OOS, negative past h=30) while U56 and ETF36 are still RISING at h=84 (1.081 / 1.061) — opposite slopes, same ordering in the IS window. Holding-episode persistence at M is 1.90 on SMALL439 vs 4.18 on U56 and 5.71 on ETF36, so the cadence dial is a far bigger intervention on the small panel. Rule 8: the pre-registered constant beats both fitted selectors on all three families at every rung and on U56/ETF is within 0.0003-0.0215 of the ORACLE. 4b 87->59->30 rows over 0/10/25 bps with every fixed-panel pass being U56 and SMALL439/ETF36 0/7 at all rungs. The 4b verdict INVERTS the U56 Sharpe ranking (6W best Sharpe but fails DD at every rung; M passes 4b at 0/10/25) — memo'd, single-universe, NOT proposed. Universe clause written in the result. Controls: 805/805 rows vs idea 175 at 7.1e-15, cost additivity 0.000e+00. 5 of 7 predictions hit. No RULES change, no new book. [2026-09-05_why-the-small-panel-wants-M-and-the-large-caps-6W_cloud.py]

207. how-many-draws-does-clause-11b-need (2026-09-05, cloud) — **ANSWERED: the queue's premise is half wrong, and the number only exists once the statistic is fixed. No RULES change, no new book, no KEEP candidate.** 90 configurations x 400 circular rotations x 2 cost rungs = **72,000 null evaluations**; idea 191's machinery imported verbatim and idea 201's published bands reproduced first at **9.7e-17** with its 3-block flip count exact at **32/180 = 17.8%**. **(1) The MAX band DRIFTS:** 0.1686 -> 0.2797 (**+65.9%**) and clear rate **15.6% -> 2.2%** from K=20 to K=200, monotone in both, because `max` of K draws is the (K/(K+1)) order statistic — K sets the test's SIZE (4.8% at K=20, 0.50% at K=200), not its precision, and the band's ABSOLUTE sd does not fall at all (0.0502 -> 0.0492). **(2) Its stability at K=200 is degenerate** — only 4 of 180 rows clear anything, so the flip rate falls (31.7% -> 2.8% of configs, on 20/8/4/2 disjoint blocks) by the test ceasing to fire. **(3) A fixed Q95 band is the fix:** level stable (+6.7%), sd **0.0242 -> 0.0076 = 1/3.18 against sqrt(10) = 3.16**, i.e. exactly 1/sqrt(K). **(4) THE NUMBER IS 100**, with Q95: pairwise flip 2.5% (7.7% at K=20), undetermined zone |margin| < **0.0097** (0.0921 at K=20), 16.1% of the corpus inside it; K=200 doubles the cost for no measured gain. Idea 201's `|margin| < 0.05` rule is right at K=20 and an order of magnitude too loose at Q95/K=100. **(5) Rule 8: TWELFTH consecutive do-nothing win** — S0 0.7766, all EIGHT clause-gated selectors below it at every K and both statistics (best −0.0145 t −1.89, abstaining 14/18; worst −0.0652), ORACLE +0.0431, SPY OOS 15.45%/0.8820/−33.72%. **(6) Both KEEP paths: 4a 37/180, 4b 28/180, SMALL439 0/60 on 4b (twelfth reproduction of idea 136), and ALL 28 4b passes are inside their own 200-draw band** — a tenfold draw increase rescues none. 4 of 6 predictions hit; the two misses are the K=200 flip-rate comparisons, which rest on one block pair per configuration. Memo carries the proposed clause 11b wording. Ideas 214-216 queued. Script `research/backtests/2026-09-05_how-many-draws-does-clause-11b-need_cloud.py`; `.result.md`, `.memo.md`, `.band.csv`, `.flip.csv`, `.zone.csv`, `.walkforward.csv`, `.keep.csv` alongside. **[COMPANION RUN, same day, cloud lane: `2026-09-05_clause-11b-draw-count-by-enumeration_cloud`. Same 90 configurations, but the rotation population ENUMERATED IN FULL (84,600 backtests, 169,020 null rows) instead of a 400-draw pool. Confirms: max-of-n size = 1/(n+1) exactly (0.04762/0.01961/0.00990/0.00498), 4a 37/180 and 4b 28/180 identical, S0 do-nothing 0.7766 identical, 0 of 8 gate settings beat do-nothing. Corrects two things: the population is J-1 = 974/974/869 (not ~670/~600), so a 400-draw pool is 41-46% of it; and 'K=200 buys nothing over K=100' was an artefact of one disjoint block pair — under the exact law flip-vs-truth falls 0.0312 -> 0.0238 and the undetermined zone 0.0319 -> 0.0204 from n=100 to n=200, so 100 is a FLOOR not an optimum and full enumeration (2.4x a 400-draw pool) is exact. Proposes the level-alpha permutation p-value as the exact-inference form of the sibling's Q95. 3 of 5 predictions hit.]**

201. the-margin-column-instead-of-two — idea 191 found on-share and |dSharpe| both rise with firing frequency and cancel (rho +0.656 vs +0.595), so neither is interpretable alone and it proposed publishing the pair. Test the one-number alternative directly: is the clause MARGIN (|dSharpe| - band) a sufficient statistic for the pair, i.e. does adding on-share to a margin-only reading change any published verdict? If not, PROTOCOL should ask for one column, not two. Cheap; max 2 params. (2026-09-05) — **SPLIT (2026-09-05, cloud); the queue's proposal is a KILL.** MARGIN is a sufficient statistic for the clause VERDICT — trivially, since `clears` is DEFINED as |dSharpe| > band: sign(margin) reproduces **180/180** published verdicts on Sharpe and **180/180** on drawdown, and on-share changes none. It is NOT sufficient for anything a reader does with the clause: conditional on the IS margin, on-share still predicts the OOS outcome (partial rho **-0.307** on OOS margin, **-0.361** on OOS dSharpe at t -2.37; within-family dR2 **+0.240 DDCTL / +0.217 SLEEVE**), and adding it to a margin-only rule-8 selector changes **8 of 18 picks** worth **+0.0717** OOS Sharpe. So PROTOCOL keeps idea 191's PAIR. An INVERSION of 191's framing: the MARGIN argmax is the worst of eight arms (-0.1045, t -2.73) while on-share-only is the only arm not below the do-nothing control (+0.0053, t +0.74, inside noise) — eleventh consecutive do-nothing win, S0 0.7766. TWO BY-PRODUCTS bigger than the answer: (1) measured on 3 disjoint 20-draw blocks the null band's own range is **63.3% of its value** and **17.8% of clause verdicts flip** between blocks, concentrated where |margin| < 0.05 — clause 11b needs an error bar; (2) idea 191's rotation seed is `SEED + hash((panel,family,thr)) % 10000` and Python salts hash() on str, so its committed **BANDS are irreproducible** (its 180 real rows reproduce at 2.220e-16) — a live PROTOCOL-5 violation. 2 of 5 pre-registered predictions hit.

194. abstention-is-the-only-thing-that-ever-helps (2026-09-05, cloud) — **ANSWERED: idea 194's hypothesis is HALF RIGHT IN THE STRONGEST POSSIBLE WAY. Report-only rule-8 clause 8b proposed; no new book; RULES untouched.** 378-book corpus rebuilt by importing idea 177's `build_panels()`/`build_base()`; CELL = (panel, cost, share) = 54 cells, MENU = the 7 keys, CONTROL = NONE (apply no key); exactly 2 tuned params (abstention threshold τ x 5, IS selector x 5) = 25 gates, all reported.
