Both KEEP paths: 4a 62/204, **4b 4/204** and the only clearing-and-positive 4b pass is the ORACLE FWDRET/POS. **(5) Rule 8 (12 cells, key chosen ≤2016-12-31): the eight-run do-nothing streak ENDS but every large win is leaky** — C-DVOL/NEG +0.809, C-PRICE/NEG +0.450, S1 +0.403, S2 +0.372 vs leak-free **S2LF +0.137 (t +2.19 cost-collapsed, 11W/1L)**; S3 clause+positive +0.014 abstaining 10 of 12; S0 0.6061, SPY OOS 0.8820. **4 of 7 pre-registered predictions HIT (P2/P4/P7 missed).** No new book, no KEEP; RULES untouched. 159. the-share-at-which-ranking-stops-paying (2026-09-05, lane B) — **ANSWERED, KILL of the question: there is NO share at which ranking stops paying, because the value and the cost of a tilt are the SAME function of share.** A crossing needs d = slope(log g) - slope(log c) < 0; bootstrapped (block 21d, 2000 reps, seed 159) d straddles zero in **6 of 6** (panel, tilt) cells (u56 INV +0.032 [-1.186,+1.097], broad INV +0.173 [-0.781,+1.274]). Only **10 of 36** cells give a finite empirical m* and **6 of those sit at the grid endpoint** m=0.05; the fitted estimator returns -16.72/+15.82/+86.90, the signature of parallel lines. Mechanism is arithmetic: both value and cost scale with the names the tilt moves, which is what share governs (idea 153) — so g/c is flat (u56 **1.95/1.83/2.20** at m=0.05/0.53/1.00, Spearman(m,ratio) -0.224). On the HONEST cost bar (exact incremental cost) g/c = **16-4254** on u56 and 9-193 on broad at every share: at the incumbent's own m=0.53 the live INV tilt has **2.68 pp/yr** of magnitude against **0.083 pp/yr** of cost — **it is never too expensive, it is wrong-signed everywhere** (negative at all 10 shares on u56, -13.49 to -0.61 pp/yr, and all 10 on broad). 5th independent delete-the-scaler confirmation, and the first to separate cost from direction. Rule 8: the m*-gate returns mean OOS Sharpe **0.7212** vs **0.8044** for doing nothing (wins 1 of 12) and 0.7005 for plain IS-Sharpe; all four selectors lose to SPY 0.882. Reproduction 8 of 8 EXACT (incl. the cost-derivation identity at 0.00e+00). 4a 22/90, 4b 17/90 lit and 24/90 norm at 10 bps, **0/90 at 25 bps**; no new book, nothing proposed. 3 of 6 predictions held; P3 the informative failure. 159. the-share-at-which-ranking-stops-paying (2026-09-05, cloud — INDEPENDENT CONCURRENT RUN, written and executed in parallel with the lane-B entry above, which pushed first; disjoint implementations, SAME verdict) — **ANSWERED, KILL of the proposed PROTOCOL clause: the cost-based number exists (q* ~ 0.85) and cannot bind, and the bar that does bind is a random key.** Idea 153's grid reproduces EXACTLY (63 cells, max|diff| 2.2e-16) and its slope widens to 6 keys on a 14-point share grid (Spearman -0.986/-0.987/-0.947). cost_tilt is 1-30 bps of CAGR against |dCAGR| of 26-480 bps, so q*(R1) lands at 0.84-0.95 (bootstrap p05-p95 0.79-0.96) — outside any tradable share, family-dependent (POW never crosses in 0/400 draws, LOG in 20-68%) and unstable across the rule-8 split. Rule 8: ranking below q* LOSES to not ranking (-0.045 OOS Sharpe, wins 1/3), and on broad the IS-Sharpe selector picked RND at q=1.00 — the equal-weight-all-eligible book — for the run's best OOS Sharpe. 164. is-4b-internally-consistent-at-any-vol (2026-09-05, cloud) — **ANSWERED, KILL of the mis-pairing hypothesis: the band is NOT empty, and the coefficient that binds is GAMMA, not delta.** Solved: `v in [gamma/s, delta/rho]`, non-empty iff `rho/s <= delta/gamma = 0.857`. Frame verified (`rho > delta` predicts idea 156's v=1 DD failure in 100.0% of its 300 books; analytic vs exact ladder agree 95.8%). 12 of 24 books admit a legal gross, 7 clear all five bars (bands 1-3 ladder points of 0.10 gross); the SMALL panel is empty 8/8 at rho/s 1.35-3.87. Idea 156's premise reproduces (298/300 DD failures at v=1) but its inference reverses: 72.3% of those books have a non-empty band, v=1 just sits above the right edge. Coefficients: delta saturates at 0.65 (14/24 flat to 1.15), gamma bites to 1.00 (0/24); +1.26 books per 0.05 of gamma vs +0.70 of delta. Rule 8: the band is a DRAWDOWN instrument only (OOS pass 41.7% vs 20.8% do-nothing, -0.001 Sharpe, -3.7pp MaxDD) and does not beat a cap-only control; IS->OOS transfer weak (Spearman +0.585, 75% verdict agreement). No new KEEP.

93. absorbing-state-audit-of-every-state-dependent-rule — idea 22 found that any exposure rule whose RE-ENTRY condition is a function of the book's own equity (new high, recovery to -D/2) is ABSORBING at zero exposure, and near-absorbing at small k. The project has several such rules on the books (idea 40's book-level DD control, idea 9's per-name trailing stop, idea 75's conditionally-armed stop). Audit each for the same failure and propose the general fix: re-entry conditioned on an EXOGENOUS series (SPY's own 200d, breadth) rather than on the book's equity. Max 2 params. (2026-09-04, lane C) — **DONE 2026-09-05 lane B: ANSWERED — pathology CONFIRMED and generalised, its diagnosis CORRECTED, the proposed exogenous fix KILLED as an improvement.** Equity-conditioned re-entry at k=0 is EXACTLY absorbing (140/140 armed arms never released, mean capture 724 rebalances); episode length ~1/k; exogenous release cures it (0 of 288 absorbed) but is Sharpe-identical to the pathology (-0.263 vs -0.264) and makes MaxDD DEEPER in 140/288. P5 falsified: the operative property is a HIGH-WATER MARK, not endogeneity — `nhigh` (name's own peak) blocks 251 days at release rate 0.951. 0 of 1824 arms pass both KEEP paths; 0 of 12 cells converts a 4b failure into a pass; rule-8 S1 mean OOS Sharpe vs control -0.171 over 65 material moves. No RULES change, no new KEEP.

121. liquidity-screened-small-panel — **ANSWERED; a $1M ADV floor proposed as PROTOCOL clause 10 (reporting requirement + stated default, NOT a `load_universe` change). It deletes ALL 7 of the small panel's 4a passes and changes no 4b (there are none: 0 of 192 points at any floor). But the panel's two CITED findings SURVIVE the screen** (2026-09-05, cloud). Pre-registered capacity criterion, ADV only, no return input: the smallest ladder floor at which $10M trades <= 10% of the p25 held-name 20d median dollar volume in the ranked n=20 book -> **$1M** (unscreened that book trades **17.6%**; R10 46%, R5 **137%**; equal-weight books never bind at 0.1-1.9%). **(1) The floor's price is the panel's return**: EWall g=0.75 goes 10.18%/5.92%/1.64%/-4.92% CAGR and 0.678/0.413/0.181/-0.163 Sharpe on 348/252/141/44 names at none/$1M/$5M/$20M — reproducing idea 120's no-rank control to 3dp — and that decay is CONFOUNDED with survivorship, since the missing delisted cohort is the thin cohort the floor removes. **(2) P1 REFUTED — Claim A survives**: the gate's cost (ideas 49/51/39's -5.4 pp/yr) is -6.52/-4.87/-4.03/-3.56 pp by floor with dSharpe -0.34..-0.26, **negative in 12/12 cells** and -3.70 pp at 0 bps; it attenuates 25%, not by half, and never flips sign. The small-cap trend inversion is not a sub-$1M-ADV artefact. **(3) P2 REFUTED — Claim B's ordering holds exactly at the $1M floor** (none 0.358 > 200d 0.348 > vol60 0.323 > both 0.312), with inversions only at $0 (vol60<both) and $5M (200d<vol60). The sharper finding is orthogonal: the **spread is 0.080 here vs 0.356 as published**, i.e. the four gates are 4x closer together under gross-matched weights (idea 81) — a weighting-convention exposure, not a liquidity one, and it belongs on the queue. **(4) 7 of 48 verdicts move**, all 4a True->False (EWall g0.50; R5u/R10u g0.50; R20u and R40u at g0.50 and 0.75), 4a by floor 7/0/0/0 — six of seven are idea 119/120's ungated unscaled family, which dies at $1M not $5M. **(5) P3 + P4 CONFIRMED**: 0 of 192 points pass 4b at any floor, rule-8's S1 picks give OOS Sharpe 0.286/0.163/-0.026/-0.608 against SPY's 0.882 (monotonically worse as the floor rises), and the drawdown-capped selector S2 is EMPTY at every floor >= $1M. Costs: only EWall (1.7-2.6x turnover) is 25-bps-robust; EWgate is -1.07% CAGR at 25 bps and the $1M floor. Result: `2026-09-05_liquidity-screened-small-panel_cloud.result.md`, `.memo.md`.
117. crisis-depth-as-the-price-denominator — **PREMISE CONFIRMED on large caps, REFUTED on small; the episode-level replacement PASSES its pre-registered bar and goes to PROTOCOL as a reporting clause, not a selector. No RULES change, no new book KEEP** (2026-09-05, lane B — claimed instead of the queue's literal last Open row 118, which commit 90c57fd had already put in flight as `2026-09-05_why-ddctl-is-cheap-on-small_cloud.py` without moving it here). Idea 94's module imported, not re-implemented: engine diff 0.0, and **idea 97's published headline reproduces exactly** (u56 gate tier 4.108 IS / 0.404 OOS vs levers 1.002/0.616) before anything was read. 3 panels x 3 books x 2 cost rungs = 18 cells, 306 arm-points and a 19-point gross ladder each, all reported; TWO tuned params (episode threshold theta, all of 8/10/15% reported; instrument family in the selector). Idea 62's episode classification built here (9 SPY episodes at theta 10% on u56/broad, 7 on small): **the IS window's -22.1% "worst crisis" is E1, a 27-day fragment of the GFC the 2009-01-13 slice merely clips**, against OOS's 2020 (-33.7%) and 2022 (-24.5%). **(1) P1 CONFIRMED on large caps:** log(price) on log(window SPY MaxDD) slopes **-4.04 u56** (t -5.78, R2 0.68) and -3.05 broad, so a deeper window makes every instrument look cheaper; **REFUTED on the small panel (+0.13, t 0.12)** where the price is just as unstable for reasons that are not depth. Honest limit: two distinct depths per panel, so these are IS/OOS contrasts, not elasticities. Median IS/OOS ratio over the 18 cells is 2.18x — idea 97's 10.2x pair is near the top of the distribution, not typical. **(2) P2 CONFIRMED — the fix works.** Pricing per crisis (premium on CALM days only, divided by the drawdown saved inside each episode, medianed within a 20 pp SHALLOW/DEEP bin) cuts median |log10(IS/OOS)| from **0.455 to 0.184** (bar was 0.227) and is more portable in **92 of 109 arms** (sign-test z +7.18; 36/42 u56, 30/33 broad, 26/34 small). **The exact object idea 97 published — the u56 gate tier — goes from a 10.2x IS/OOS swing to 0.93x within DEEP episodes and 1.34x within SHALLOW ones: the whole gap was a depth-mix effect.** **(3) P3 CONFIRMED, and it is the mechanism:** protect(e) on SPY episode depth is **+0.161 pp/pp, t +15.07** over 2,400 arm-episodes, so a whole-window denominator is a depth in disguise. Tier-by-tier: ddctl **+0.320** (t 11.6, R2 0.31, the steepest — directly relevant to open idea 118), gate +0.179, ebud +0.062, **stop +0.012 (t +1.46): the per-name trailing stop's protection does not respond to crisis depth at all**, a mechanical and newly falsifiable version of idea 94/97's "the stop is the dearest tier". Dearest episode is **E5 (2018-02, -10.1% in 9 days) at 9.15 on u56**; cheapest E7 (2020) at 0.37 — a factor of 25 between crises inside the same price list. **(4) P4 CONFIRMED — do not adopt it as a selector.** Rule-8 `Sdepth` (argmin IS depth-matched episode price) loses to idea 94's `S1`: mean OOS regret **+0.425 vs +0.337**, rank-1 4/18 vs 8/18, dOOS Sharpe -0.016, same pick 8/18. Its one signed difference is buying more drawdown for less return (OOS 7.4%/0.713/-19.5% vs S1 8.2%/0.729/-21.3%; 4a 10/18 vs 5/18), which is a worse trade under 4b's CAGR floor. Both sit far below SPY OOS 15.5%/0.882 and above live v1 4.9%/0.451. **(5) P5 CONFIRMED — no new candidate.** 4b @10 bps: u56 10 arms, broad 4 (`band3-rw, g200-rw, v1gate-rw, vol60-dg` — idea 94's published pair reproduced plus two), **small 0, none on all three panels**; @25 bps 6/2/0. 4a 3/11/12 including the small-panel control, the known pathology. **Recommendation to the Sunday review (no file modified):** amend PROTOCOL rule 4 — a drawdown price must be quoted per crisis episode at a stated depth with the premium measured on calm days, and a whole-window-MaxDD price may not be compared across windows or panels; plus the stop's non-response to depth as a citable clause. Caveats: survivorship on all three current-constituent panels (flatters gates), calm-day annualisation is a premium proxy not a tradeable return, 9 episodes at the point theta is a small sample, and the small panel's P1 refutation is unexplained (belongs with idea 118). Result: `2026-09-05_crisis-depth-as-the-price-denominator_B.result.md`, `.memo.md`.

113. crisis-beta-as-the-pre-registrable-selector — **KILL** (2026-09-05, lane C). beta_IS(p) = -slope(d_y ~ SPY_MaxDD_y) on the 7 IS years; S_crisis(B) = argmax [IS Sharpe + beta_IS*B], B in {0.05,0.10,0.20} (B=0 IS rule 8), plus a sign-flipped falsification control, across idea 99/109's same 44 cells. Every budget LOSES: mean OOS Sharpe 0.960 vs rule 8's 1.048, dOOS **-0.088** = 5.9x the entire +0.0150 ceiling (ceiling independently reproduced), 4b passes 20 -> 15/14/14, and the dial turned BACKWARDS does better (-0.025). Mechanisms: T1 beta_IS -> beta_OOS slope only +0.172 (mean 2.80 -> 0.378, a 6x shrinkage the selector never applies, IS fit R^2 ~0.5-5% on n=7); T2 beta_IS -> d_OOS r -0.604 / rho +0.159 (signs disagree) vs rule 8's own input d_IS -> d_OOS r +0.850 / rho +0.703. Structural defect: beta_IS is an unbounded superlinear function of overlay DOSE (sleeve f: 0 -> 2.6 -> 7.1 -> 14.3 -> 31.8), so any B>0 saturates at the grid endpoint — sleeve grid damage -0.460. Realised budget B* = +0.027 (OOS annual SPY MaxDD -13.8% vs IS -11.1%); shrunk, the right coefficient is ~0.005, an order of magnitude below the smallest tested. Quarterly-beta robustness arm agrees. No RULES/PROTOCOL change; recommend closing the selector line. `2026-09-05_crisis-beta-as-the-pre-registrable-selector_C.result.md`
119. V1u-small-negative-price — **KILL the citation as worded** (2026-09-05, cloud — INDEPENDENT REPLICATION, written and run in parallel with lane B's run of the same idea; the two agree to the decimal on every shared number, which is the strongest form of confirmation this project has). Idea 97's 32 `small/V1u` pricelist rows reproduced EXACTLY (1.8e-15) before anything was read. (a) The queue's own wording is not literal: 11 negative of 13 priceable of 20 gate rows, `band3-dg` prices POSITIVE at both rungs and both `vol60` arms buy exactly zero drawdown. (b) DECISIVE: the control book draws down -34.0% and every negative-priced arm buys 0.05-3.28 pp of drawdown, i.e. 0.15-9.7% of the book's own depth — **0 of 11 negative rows are material** under the pre-registered 10%-of-control bar, and a 3 pp floor leaves one gate row in the entire cell. The headline -1.41 is a ratio of noise. (c) 200-draw name-subsample bootstrap: ARTEFACT in 7 of 12 audits; the three arms whose CAGR sign survives resampling are all `-rw`, which is idea 94's SELECTION convention, not insurance — every de-gross arm fails (sign held 52-82% of draws). (d) It is not one year (0 sign flips across 96 arm-years) and not costs (gain present at 0 bps, turnover 32.8-33.5x vs control 33.3x); it IS largely top-5 concentration (negative arms 5/6 -> 4/8 -> 4/9 -> 2/9 as n goes 5->10->20->40). (e) Rule 8 picks `g200-rw`: 8.7%/0.634/-32.8%, OOS 8.9%/0.631 vs SPY OOS 15.5%/0.882; **0 of 14 audited books pass 4a or 4b**. Survivorship runs AGAINST the artefact hypothesis and is stated as such. Proposal to the Sunday review: replace idea 94's absolute priceability floor (dMaxDD > 0.10 pp) with a relative one (>= 10% of the control's |MaxDD|) — queued as idea 120. Result: `2026-09-05_V1u-small-negative-price_cloud.result.md`. CROSS-CHECK vs lane B: identical premise count (6 priceable / 5 negative @10 bps), identical rule-8 pick and OOS row (`g200-rw` 8.9%/0.631/-32.8% vs v1 7.9%/0.581 and SPY 15.5%/0.882), same concentration direction, same KILL. ADDITIVE here: the pre-registered materiality bar (0 of 11 negative rows material; a 3 pp floor leaves one), the `-rw` vs `-dg` split in the bootstrap (every arm whose CAGR sign survives resampling is the SELECTION convention, every insurance-form arm fails), the 0-bps decomposition (the gain is not a cost saving), and the relative-floor proposal (idea 123). ADDITIVE there: the vol-scaler attribution (/sqrt(vol20) costs 11.7 pp/yr at n=5) and the investability bound.

The NUMERATOR is robust (dCAGR<0 at all five costs, 61/80 sub-panels, 0/16 LOYO sign flips) and is fully explained: the 200d gate changes the book on **66 of 817 dates (8.1%)**, ~77 of 4,074 held name-dates (**1.9%**), deleting the picks with median vol20 **0.095** vs 0.173 kept — i.e. it repairs a sliver of the damage done by /sqrt(vol20), which itself picks names at vol20 0.173 against a panel median of 0.399 and **costs 11.7 pp/yr of CAGR** at n=5 (7.05% -> 18.72%, 0.537 -> 0.690 Sharpe, turnover 33.3x -> 21.5x). Queue hypotheses: turnover **REFUTED** for the numerator / confirmed for the denominator; concentration **CONFIRMED** (3/3 negative at n=5, 0/3 at n=40) with the scaler the bigger and unnamed half (0 of 6 priceable cells negative with it off); survivorship **untestable from inside the panel and directionally exculpatory** (the missing names are the ones a gate excludes, so their absence flatters the ungated control). Also NOT small-panel specific: `g200-rw` prices -1.414/-0.184/-0.118 on small/u56/broad — a correction to idea 97's C1 framing. And uninvestable: held-name p25 dollar volume $0.87M, p50 $4.33M, so $10M of capital is 34.6% of median ADV. **4b 0/43 arm-points, 4a 0/11**; rule 8 picks `g200-rw` under both selectors, OOS 8.86%/0.631/-32.8% vs live-v1 7.92%/0.581/-32.8% vs SPY 15.45%/0.882/-33.7%. No RULES change. Follow-ups 120-122. `2026-09-05_v1u-small-negative-price_B.result.md`

Labels are a price-only annual correlation proxy for GICS (11.0%/12.5% YoY churn), so this rejects correlation-cluster caps, not GICS caps. **(4) Pre-registered prediction Q4 REFUTED**: the eligibility gate tops out at +0.042 on the primary universe, below idea 84's own 0.05 threshold — the gate is a drawdown instrument (no-gate MaxDD -26.0% vs -20.1%), not a Sharpe instrument. **Deliverable — instruments that are NOT dials on a Sharpe-bound book**: gross, turnover budget (idea 84), per-name weight cap, sector cap, eligibility gate. The only dial is the selection itself. Proposed PROTOCOL note: "when a candidate fails 4b on a Sharpe bar, no exposure, turnover, weight-cap or sector-cap setting will fix it — change the selection or abandon the book." By-product: composite ties make `rank <= 20` select 21 names on 22/4698 broad days (45/4698 u56), so idea 2's literal `g/n` book runs at 0.7875 gross there. No new candidate; the winner is idea 84's standing `ew-band3`. Memo written. (`2026-09-04_sharpe-bound-book-change_cloud.py`)

84. which-4b-bar-binds-decides-the-lever — **KILL idea 83's two-branch lever rule as worded; replacement one-line clause proposed and measured; KEEP-candidate by-product** (2026-09-04, lane B). 480 grid points + a 312-point fine gross ladder, ALL reported; harness reproduces `engine.backtest` to 0.000e+00 and the published control rows of ideas 2, 46, 57 and 72 exactly. **(1) The census is degenerate:** the CAGR floor binds 6 of 8 (candidate, universe) cells at 10 bps, the DD cap 1 (C2 on u56) and a Sharpe bar 1 (C2 on broad, H2), and it is stable in neither cost (C2 flips DD->H1 on u56 between 10 and 25 bps, DD->H2 on broad between 5 and 10) nor window (IS-only and full-sample binding bars disagree in 3 of 8). **(2) The mechanism kills the two branches:** the Sharpe bars are g-invariant in all 24 (cell, cost) triples — full-sample Sharpe spread <= 0.0061 over g = 0.40->1.00, reproducing idea 66 on four books — so gross is ONE axis carrying BOTH non-Sharpe bars (~+1.4pp CAGR and -2.0pp MaxDD per +0.10 of g at dSharpe 0.000), and 'DD-bound -> cut g' / 'CAGR-bound -> raise g' are the same lever in two directions. **(3) Idea 83's CAGR branch names the wrong instrument:** the entry-only budget is CAGR-neutral by construction (saves cost, raises cash — broad EWall B=0.20 cuts turnover 8.27x->7.80x worth 0.05pp while gross falls 0.750->0.742 worth -0.11pp), giving d_CAGR +0.01..+0.20pp against the 0.26pp gap; gross gives +3.7pp. Its real content is Sharpe (+0.047 C46/broad, +0.050 C72/broad) and MaxDD. Idea 83's SIGN was right (12/12 cut-gross arms lower the CAGR margin), its REMEDY wrong. **(4) The branch idea 83 lacks:** on the H2-bound cell m_H2 spans -0.119..-0.020 over 20 arms and never turns positive (best improving move +0.0027, 0 conversions) — no lever fixes a Sharpe-bound book. Pre-registered P3 was worded symmetrically and is FALSIFIED as worded (g1.00/B0.10 moves H2 by -0.099) but only downward; reported both ways. **(5) Replacement clause, measured:** since both non-Sharpe bars sit on the gross axis, 4b defines an INTERVAL [g_min, g_max] per (book, universe, cost) and a book passes iff the interval is non-empty AND its g-invariant Sharpe bars pass. Only `C57/ew-band3` has both on both universes at all three costs; cross-universe window [0.75,0.90] at 5/10 bps and [0.80,0.90] at 25. **(6) KEEP-candidate: `ew-band3` at g=0.85** — u56 12.8%/1.136/-17.1% (halves 1.113/1.160, OOS 14.4%/1.234), broad 12.6%/1.064/-18.9% (halves 1.163/0.971, OOS 12.7%/1.073), 5.5x/5.8x turnover; the project's first arm passing 4b full AND OOS on BOTH universes at 5, 10 AND 25 bps (cross-universe full+OOS passes: 29/80, 18/80, 6/80), 4a on broad. This ANSWERS idea 58: the published g=0.75 dies at 25 bps on the CAGR floor (10.5%/10.3% vs a 10.68% bar) and gross is precisely the CAGR lever. g=0.85 is rule-8 selected on both universes by the 4b-aware IS rule (argmax-IS-Sharpe picks g=1.00, which breaks broad's DD cap). **(7) The rule under test has no net content:** mean OOS Sharpe RBIND 1.098 vs argmax-IS 1.097 vs the published control 1.083; RBIND avoids one -0.070 pick and forgoes one +0.060. Memo written. Ideas 90-92 queued. (`2026-09-04_which-4b-bar-binds_B.py`)

(`2026-09-04_selectability-pre-test_cloud.py`)
89. is-window-has-no-crash — **KILL the amendment; premise REFUTED** (2026-09-04, lane C). 66 grid points, 36 selections (3 splits x 12 universe-grid cells), ALL reported. Moving the split changes 8/12 picks (S13) and 5/12 (S21), only 2/12 stable across all three — but after idea 87's 8a selectability pre-test only 3/8 and 2/5 of those sit on a grid with anything to select on, and every one of them moves realised 2022+ Sharpe by <=0.034 (mean 2022+ Sharpe of the 12 picks: S13 0.954, S16 0.924, S21 0.924). **The premise is backwards:** S13 and S16 share the identical IS MaxDD (-22.1%, 2011) yet picks move, so crash content is not the cause; and putting COVID in-sample (S21) makes 4b's DD cap LOOSER (bar -13.2% -> -20.2%, arms clearing it 27->30 on u56, 19->24 on broad) because the cap is relative to SPY on the same window and trend-gated books fall less than SPY. **The binding IS constraint is the CAGR floor** (u56 4/33 -> 17/33 -> 23/33), not the DD cap. Incumbent S16 selects best of the three (mean regret -0.028 vs -0.059/-0.046, rho(IS,OOS) +0.361 vs +0.205/-0.228; S21's is negative — the longest IS window selects worst), though its OOS window is the one this repo's history was produced under, so the honest claim is 'no alternative split is better'. By-products: rule-8 selection is DEGENERATE on any grid whose parameter has no data in the IS window (crypto caps under S13 — identical IS Sharpe to 4dp, and the source of the only four large consequences in the run); idea 2's n=20 on u56 is picked only under S16 (S13/S21 pick n=40, which fails 4b on CAGR) although 2022+ Sharpe is 1.100 vs 1.099. (`2026-09-04_is-window-has-no-crash_C.py`)
79. rank-hysteresis-as-the-turnover-lever — **ANSWERED by idea 86** (2026-09-04, lane B): k in {1.25,1.5,2} on CAND20, both universes. Cuts turnover 40-60% (9.63 -> 3.91x/yr at k=2 on u56) with positive dCAGR in 6/6 cells and non-negative dMaxDD in 5/6, but t(dSharpe) only 0.00..+1.70 — cost-neutral, not significantly better. gm-invariant to 0.001. `CAND20/hyst2` is a 4b KEEP-candidate on u56, fails broad on H2 (0.831 vs 0.837). Memo written; needs idea 45's cost/lag sweep before any RULES change. (`2026-09-04_gross-matched-turnover-constraints_B.py`)
86. gross-matched-turnover-constraints — **KILL for `budget-top`** (1/12 cells survive; sign flips +1.53pp -> -5.74pp CAGR, dGross +0.043, and gm demotes it to the control in 3/3 rule-8 picks it won); `budget-pro`, `cadence` (idea 3) and `hyst` (idea 79) all survive gross-matching to <=0.003 dGross. By-product: idea 79's `CAND20/hyst2` is a 4b KEEP-candidate on u56 (12.7%/1.145/-17.2%, OOS 1.231, TO 3.9x vs the standing candidate's 9.6x). (2026-09-04, lane B, `2026-09-04_gross-matched-turnover-constraints_B.py`)
87. interior-4b-passes-are-unselectable — **KILL the amendment** (2026-09-04, cloud). 66 grid points, 48 selections, ALL reported. Margin-aware selection Rm(m) does NOT beat rule 8: OOS Sharpe vs the default arm +0.027/+0.029/+0.033 at m=0/1/2pp vs R0's +0.031, it never reaches the grid's OOS-best (0/10, 0/9, 0/4 vs R0's 2/12), and 13 of 48 selections are INFEASIBLE (8 of 12 at m=2pp). Reason: the two grids that produced the interior passes cannot be selected on at all — a 2x change in gross moves IS Sharpe by 0.0002-0.0046 (IS MaxDD by 7-9pp), and the crypto grid's IS MaxDD spread is exactly 0.0000 on both universes, so Rm recovers idea 15's c=0.05 in 0 of 8 cells. Confirms idea 57's band book (all 4 non-zero bands pass cross-universe 4b; every feasible rule picks 0.08) and idea 2's universe-specificity (n=20 on u56, n=30 on broad). Proposes PROTOCOL 8a selectability pre-test.
16. monthly-seasonality — **KILL** (2026-09-04, cloud). 144 points, all reported; 24 improve on control (best +0.063, top20 +0.003), rule 8 loses 0/6 (-0.29..-0.57 OOS Sharpe). Load-bearing number: Spearman(H1 month means, H2 month means) = +0.000/-0.182/-0.364/-0.021 (u56) and +0.000/-0.049/-0.322/+0.028 (broad) — month ordering does not persist, so no mask is estimable. Sep is worst full-sample everywhere but chosen by 0 of 6 in-sample worst-1 fits.
19. spy-tlt-gld-riskparity — **KILL as a book; ADOPT as the standing benchmark bar** (2026-09-04, lane C). All 12 grid points (L in {20,60,120,252} x g in {0.60,0.75,1.00}) reported plus EW-thirds and 60/40 controls; `compare()` cross-check reproduces the harness to the decimal. **0/12 on 4a, 0/12 on 4b**, failure modes 8x`CAGR` and 4x`DD,CAGR` and nothing else — every point beats SPY's Sharpe in both halves (H1 1.02-1.11 vs 0.957, H2 0.91-0.93 vs 0.837) and OOS (0.98-1.01 vs 0.884). **The 4b failure is structural:** Sharpe is flat in `g` to +/-0.002 across 0.40-1.40 (a fourth confirmation of idea 66's pure-lever finding), so 4b collapses to an interval on gross — the DD cap needs g <= ~0.88, the CAGR floor needs g >= ~1.15, and **the interval is empty**; closing it requires leverage, which rule 2 forbids. Rule 8 picks `L=20,g=1.00` (IS 1.019), OOS 2017+ **9.4%/0.984/-22.7%** vs live 7.8%/0.751/-13.8% and SPY 15.5%/0.884/-33.7% — OOS Sharpe beats both, OOS CAGR is 61% of SPY's; Spearman(IS,OOS) +0.371 on a grid spanning 0.05 Sharpe, so selection barely matters. `L` is nearly inert on Sharpe (0.05 spread, all in H1) but is the turnover lever: 2.41/0.95/0.59/0.40 turns per year. **4a turns out to be purchasable by de-grossing:** at `L=60, g<=0.55` the book passes 4a outright while earning 5.0% CAGR against the live book's 6.5% — a formal pass that makes less money than the book it replaces, which is evidence about 4a (the defect 4b was added to fix, seen from the other side) and is NOT proposed as a KEEP. **The benchmark answer:** at matched drawdown (g=0.60, -14.0% vs live -13.8%) risk parity gives Sharpe 1.006 vs 0.666, H1 1.101 vs 0.641, H2 0.934 vs 0.692, OOS 1.007 vs 0.751, on **0.58 turns/yr against the live book's 23.6** — three ETFs rebalanced monthly beat the live book on every risk-adjusted axis and trail it only on CAGR, by 1.0pp, for 1/40th the trading. Inverse-vol weighting itself adds little (EW thirds 9.3%/0.983/-23.1%, ~0.02 Sharpe and 0.7pp DD worse): the diversification does the work, not the vol weighting. 60/40 SPY-TLT fails four 4b bars (H2 0.734, OOS 0.807) — GLD carries H2. Bad years 2013 -2.1%, 2015 -2.7%, 2022 -12.1% (SPY -18.2%): a duration/real-asset sleeve, not a crash hedge. Binding caveat: 2009-2026 is one duration regime and the H1->H2 decay 1.10->0.93 is it turning. Recommendation: no RULES change; carry `RP L=60,g=0.75` (6.9%/1.005/-17.2%, 0.72 turns/yr) as the bar every future candidate must beat *at matched drawdown*. Memo: research/backtests/2026-09-04_spy-tlt-gld-riskparity_C.result.md
88. vol-cap-as-a-satellite-clause — **KILL the percentile wording; satellite stays PARK** (2026-09-04, lane B). 48 points (2 universes x 3 books x [control + 7 gate specs]) at a FIXED 5% cap and `matched` funding, all reported; reproduces idea 2's KEEP row to the decimal, control equivalence 1.4e-17, percentile look-ahead check 0.000e+00. **`vol20 < 0.60` is not one rule but two, measured before any backtest:** it is p98.9 (u56) / p98.3 (broad) of the median equity name's OWN vol distribution and p99.5 of SPY's — a tail exclusion that never binds, with 8 of 55 / 8 of 134 names never touching it — but **p58.8 of BTC's and p30.2 of ETH's**, a median exclusion that binds constantly. That single asymmetry reconciles idea 15 (the cap protects a satellite) with idea 56 (the same cap destroys small caps). **On Sharpe the clause is pure cost and monotone in looseness:** `none` (200d gate only) is the best arm in 6 of 6 (book, universe) cells, mean dSharpe vs own control +0.147/+0.152 vs abs0.60's +0.072/+0.074, spec ordering Spearman +1.00/+0.89/+1.00 across universes. **But 4b is a drawdown bar and there the ordering reverses:** 9 of 24 cross-universe passes, and on the ranked book ONLY the two tightest absolute specs pass (`CAND20/abs0.45`, `CAND20/abs0.60`) — abs0.80 and all four percentile specs fail broad on `DD`, and every CAND20 failure is `DD` and nothing else. **Absolute beats percentile at matched exposure on both asset classes:** abs0.80 vs p70 (identical 35.6% either-on) gives dSharpe +0.096/+0.099 vs +0.074/+0.080 with better DD; abs0.60 beats p50 at LOWER exposure. Mechanism: an expanding percentile **ratchets** — it re-admits the instrument at ever-higher absolute vol as its own history gets more volatile, i.e. it loosens exactly when the asset gets dangerous, worth ~2pp of MaxDD at matched exposure. **The equity-side diagnostic is decisive:** rewriting the EQUITY gate as a percentile introduces a `DD` 4b failure on BOTH lists at p50/p70/p90 that NO absolute spec has (EWall p90 1.070/`DD`, p70 1.036/`DD,CAGR`, p50 0.884/`H1,DD,CAGR` on u56), so the percentile wording would break the equity book to fix a satellite clause. **Rule 8 rejects the passers, idea 87's shape again:** PROTOCOL's window picks `CAND20/p90` (u56 OOS 16.7%/1.270/-19.7%) and `EWall/p90` (broad 13.2%/1.144/-20.1%) — both clear their OOS bars but `CAND20/p90` fails full-sample cross-universe 4b on DD — and its Spearman +0.932/+0.879 is the EQUITY book's ordering, since the IS window holds ~1.5 yr of BTC and zero ETH. On the crypto-era walk-forward S1 picks `none` in 6 of 6 cells and **not one clears the OOS 4b bars** (Spearman collapses to +0.541/+0.475). IS Sharpe is monotone in looseness while the DD cap is not, so every selection rule overshoots past `abs0.45`/`abs0.60`. Cost: dSharpe WIDENS with cost (+0.133 -> +0.178 on u56 EWall) but MaxDD blows out -18.8% -> -31.4%, so no arm survives at crypto-realistic cost. Exact RULES wording (absolute, with a satellite clause and an explicit "do not re-express as a percentile") in the memo; recommended AGAINST adopting the satellite. Survivorship is the binding caveat: BTC/ETH are the two survivors and a loose clause is what would have held the dead names, so "looser is better" is biased in exactly the direction it points. Memo: research/backtests/2026-09-04_vol-cap-as-a-satellite-clause_B.result.md
89. one-year-leverage-audit — **DONE, infrastructure + one PARK-worthy finding** (2026-09-04, cloud). Generic LOYO harness (delete one calendar year, chain-link the rest, recompute every statistic AND every 4b bar on the retained days, SPY included; zero tuned parameters; reproduces ideas 2 and 72 to the decimal). 13 (book, universe) cells x 18 years. **Only 2 of 7 published 4b passes are LOYO-robust: idea 2's top20 (18/18) and idea 46's frac085 (18/18), both on universe.json; ew-band3 is 17/18 on both lists and idea 72's B136/EWall is 10/18.** But **12 of the 14 verdict flips are the BAR moving, not the book weakening** — deleting 2020 raises SPY's MaxDD cap by 5.53pp and deleting 2022 raises the CAGR floor by 1.68pp, so 4b's DD and CAGR bars are themselves one-year-levered. No equity candidate is unusually year-dependent (worst-drop dSharpe: SPY -0.059, candidates -0.071..-0.092, i.e. 1.10-1.56x the benchmark's own). **Every candidate's best year is 2022** (+7.3..+14.0pp excess) except top20/u56 (2018): the 4b edge is largely one bear market avoided. **Validation:** LOYO on idea 15's crypto arm as a PAIRED DIFFERENCE gives t +2.04 falling below 2.0 on deletion of any of 2016/2017/2020/2024 (worst 2020, t +1.48) — the harness flags it, while LOYO on the same book's own Sharpe does not, so the audit must be run on the statistic the idea is argued on. **Rule 8: LOYO-robustness is a disqualifier, not a selector** — on broad, max-IS-Sharpe picks ew-band3 (OOS 1.074 PASS) and max-IS-worst-case-LOYO picks top20 (OOS 0.894), strictly worse. Proposed PROTOCOL rule 10 with exact wording in the memo; recommended as a PARK-trigger only. Memo: research/backtests/2026-09-04_one-year-leverage-audit_cloud.result.md
14. rsi2-sleeve — **KILL** (2026-09-04, cloud). 42 points (2 universes x 3 cores [v1/top20/ew-all] x f in {0.25,0.50} x thr in {5,10,20}, 4 cost levels each). The sleeve LOSES in 36 of 36 (universe, core, f, thr) cells at 10 bps (mean dSharpe -0.412, median -0.371, paired t -2.5 to -9.5); rule 8 picks f=0 in 5 of 6 walk-forwards and the one arm it does pick (broad/top20, DD-capped rule) fails OOS. The signal is real but too small to trade: gross Sharpe 0.46/0.55/0.75 at thr=5/10/20 and entry-day forward returns 0.21%/0.09%/0.05% vs 0.063% unconditional, against 65x/113x/165x annual turnover = 6.5/11.3/16.5 pp of cost per year at PROTOCOL's 10 bps. Robust to execution lag (h-profile flat over h=0..4, so no one-day pop to forfeit). Memo not written: not a KEEP candidate.
15. crypto-sleeve — **PARK** (2026-09-04, lane C). 78 points (2 universes x 3 books [v1/CAND20/EWall] x [control + 2 funding x 2 gates x 3 caps]), all reported; harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row and the live v1 row to the decimal, c=0 equivalence 1.4e-17. **The largest and most consistent improvement the repo has produced — and rule 8 rejects it 4 of 4.** All **36 of 36** sleeve arms beat their own crypto-free control on full-sample Sharpe on BOTH lists (paired daily t +1.71..+2.81 gross-matched, +2.70..+3.21 additive), and **5 of 39 points pass cross-universe 4b at 10 bps** (`CAND20/matched/same/c5`, `EWall/matched/same/c5`, `EWall/matched/same/c10`, `EWall/matched/trend/c5`, `EWall/add/same/c5`) — the sleeve repairs exactly the bar each control was missing (CAND20's broad H2 0.814 vs 0.837; EWall's u56 CAGR 10.4% vs the 10.68% floor). The queued 10% arm on u56, `CAND20/matched/same/c10`, is 14.3%/1.178/-19.1% (halves 1.254/1.127, OOS 1.237) vs the control's 12.7%/1.093/-18.3% at 10.3x turnover, and the advantage WIDENS with cost (dSharpe +0.154 at 10 bps -> +0.276 at 100). **Why PARK: the passing arms are unreachable.** IS Sharpe is monotone in the cap and higher for the loose gate in nearly every cell, so S1 and S2 buy c=15/`trend` in 4 of 4 walk-forwards and every one FAILS the OOS drawdown bar (u56 -31.1% and broad -30.4% vs the -20.2% cap on rule 8's window; -19.4% and -24.1% vs -14.7% on a crypto-era window IS<=2021); within-book audits pick c=15 in **11 of 12** cells and **not one** of the 5 cross-universe passers is ever selected. Spearman(IS,OOS Sharpe) +0.771/+0.855 on rule 8's window collapses to **+0.362/+0.252** on the crypto era — the stable ordering is the equity book's, not the sleeve's. **Rule 8 cannot validate this idea on the data that exists:** BTC is tradeable from 2015-07-02 and ETH from 2018-08-27, so rule 8's IS window holds ~1.5 yr of BTC and ZERO ETH. **The t-stat is one year:** 2017 (BTC +1425%) sits inside H1 while only 13.1%/24.0% of H1 days hold any crypto; restricted to 2018-09+ the effect shrinks a third and loses significance (t_cry +0.66..+2.06, dSharpe negative in 4 of 18 gross-matched arms), and 2018 is the hidden counter-year (BTC -74%, ETH -82%; `CAND20/matched/trend/c10` -0.4% vs the control's +5.9%). **Survivorship is worse here than anywhere in the repo** — BTC/ETH are the two survivors, LUNA/FTT/the 2014-18 altcoin field went to zero, not to an acquirer, so every number is an upper bound on a two-ticker sleeve chosen with ten years of hindsight. **Transferable:** every sleeve 4b failure is `DD`, and `same` (v1's own vol20<0.60 applied to crypto) vs `trend` (cap waived) is the only lever that changes side of the cap — mean dMaxDD -2.9% vs -7.0% — so the 0.60 cap is a drawdown instrument whose value is conditional on the instrument's own vol distribution, the mirror image of idea 56's small-cap finding. Same failure shape as idea 66's `ew-band3 g=0.90`: an interior-parameter cross-universe pass that no IS-Sharpe rule can reach. RULES wording recorded in the memo and recommended AGAINST adoption. Memo: research/backtests/2026-09-04_crypto-sleeve_C.result.md. Ideas 87-89 queued.
83. turnover-budget-instead-of-a-cost-assumption — **KILL** (2026-09-04, lane B). 176 points (2 universes x 2 books x [3 budgets x 2 modes + control + 4 gross levels] x 4 costs), all reported, engine-equivalence 0.000e+00. A turnover budget budgets the EXITS: it makes MaxDD WORSE (mean -2.4pp pro / -5.1pp top) while gross buys drawdown at 0.677 pp CAGR/pp MaxDD (range 0.626-0.729, dSharpe -0.001). Read in one direction, raising gross buys return ~3x more efficiently (0.68 vs ~0.22 pp/pp) and Sharpe-neutrally, so the budget is dominated on its own axis. 0 of 48 broad-universe budget points pass 4b at 10 bps (1 at any cost); no arm passes 4b on both universes. Rule 8: the IS-selected B loses to its own control OOS in 2 of 4 cases and Spearman(IS,OOS) is +0.14/-0.07 on u56, so B is not selectable. One non-replicating positive: on u56 `EWall/budget-pro0.1` lifts the 4b breakeven from 7.0 to 20.0 bps and passes 4b at 10 bps where its control fails on CAGR (broad: NaN).
80. is-52w-proximity-a-short-signal — **KILL** (2026-09-04, cloud). 36 points (2 universes x [live v1 + EWall control + 4 ranking keys x n in {5,10,20,30}]), all reported; harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row and idea 13's v1 and EWall rows to the decimal. **Idea 13's negative PROX IC is a volatility proxy, measured directly.** Mean cross-sectional Spearman(PROX, vol20) among eligible names = **-0.487** (u56) / **-0.406** (broad), and in weekly Fama-MacBeth regressions on next week's return the PROX slope collapses from **-0.00298 (t -3.03) univariate to -0.00065 (t -0.65)** once vol20 is included on u56 (broad: -0.00282 t -3.43 -> -0.00161 t -2.26, slope down 43%) while vol20 keeps its own (+0.00451 t +3.90 / +0.00294 t +3.19). Same in IC space: IPROX +0.0208/+0.0252 -> vol-neutralised **IPROXn +0.0021 (t +0.25) / +0.0134 (t +2.21)**. **Inverting the key does not pay:** IPROX beats COMP in 3/8 matched-n pairs across both lists, but negatively and significantly on u56 (dSharpe -0.075..-0.135, paired t -0.53..-2.46, -1.69%/yr) and indistinguishably on broad (**t -0.09..+0.13**, -0.04%/yr); stripping the vol content makes it strictly worse (IPROX beats IPROXn **8/8**, t +1.84..+3.11; IPROXn beats COMP **0/8**). **The vol tilt itself runs the other way and is unusable:** LOWVOL (least volatile eligible) has IC -0.0426 (t -3.94) / -0.0329 (t -3.75) and loses to COMP **0/8** (-8.4%/-9.9% per yr, t -3.0..-3.8) - inside this gate HIGH vol wins the next week, which is what IPROX buys, and it still loses. **Cross-universe 4b: 0 of 18.** IPROX-n20/n30 pass 4b on broad alone (13.4%/0.98/-19.7%, 12.1%/0.97/-19.8%) but fail u56's CAGR floor (10.6%/9.5% vs 10.68%) with paired t +0.13 vs COMP - a one-list, one-parameter pass with no separation from the control. **Rule 8 rejects it:** S1 and S2 pick LOWVOL-n20 (u56, OOS 6.2%/0.853/-12.8%) and LOWVOL-n30 (broad, 5.4%/0.692/-15.0%), both fail the OOS bars; audited within each n the IS rule picks COMP in 6 of 8 cells and IPROX/IPROXn in none; Spearman(IS,OOS) +0.621/+0.185. Cost worsens it: inverting a level key bounded near 1.0 raises turnover to 20-38x/yr (IPROX) and 22-44x/yr (IPROXn) vs COMP's 9.6-21.3x, flips/ticker/yr 9.3 vs 4.4 at n=20. **Transferable:** any key measured INSIDE the eligibility gate must be regressed on vol20 before its IC is believed - the gate is itself a vol filter, so a level statistic bounded by a running max inherits -0.4..-0.5 rank correlation with vol by construction. Survivorship points against the KILL being an artefact (IPROX buys exactly the cohort delisted names would occupy, so it is flattered and still loses). See `research/backtests/2026-09-04_prox-inverted-signal_cloud.result.md`.
11. cost-sensitivity — **KILL for RULES v1** (2026-09-04, cloud). 42 points (2 universes x 3 books x {0,5,10,15,25,50,100} bps), all reported, plus an exact 0.5 bp breakeven curve (analytic r(c)=r(0)-turnover*c/1e4 verified against the engine at max |diff| 0.000e+00). Harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row and the live v1 row to the decimal. **v1 turns over 23.6x/yr (u56) / 29.4x/yr (broad) — 2-3x the candidate books — so it pays 2.36%/2.94% per year at 10 bps and decays at -0.225/-0.265 Sharpe per 10 bps vs the candidates' -0.08/-0.10.** Its CAGR goes 9.0%/9.6% at 0 bps to -3.1%/-5.4% at 50 bps with a -46.8%/-62.9% drawdown *manufactured by fees*; edge over cash dies at **36.5 / 31.0 bps**. **But cost is not what kills v1: it fails 4b at 0 bps on both lists** (u56 H1+CAGR, broad H2+OOS+CAGR) and never beats SPY on CAGR at any cost, so the answer to 'at what cost does the edge die' is 'there is no 4b edge to kill'. **The capital-relevant finding is about the standing KEEP:** cross-universe 4b (both lists) holds for idea 2's CAND20 and for EWall at 0 and 5 bps and for NEITHER at 10 bps — 4 of 21 grid points. CAND20's cross-universe breakeven is **7.5 bps** (broad H2 binds at 8, OOS at 11), EWall's **10.5 bps**. PROTOCOL's 10 bps assumption sits on top of the candidate's margin, not inside it. Rule 8: S1/S2 both pick CAND20@0bps on u56 (OOS 15.5%/1.248/-18.2%, clears every OOS bar); on broad S1 picks CAND20@0bps and S2 EWall@0bps (both clear); Spearman(IS,OOS Sharpe) +0.990/+0.962. **The pre-registered rule-8 cost question answers no:** the IS breakeven mis-estimates the OOS one by +26.0/-13.5 bps (CAND20) and +19.5/-1.0 (EWall) — a ±15-25 bp spread, wider than the candidate's whole margin. Survivorship points the wrong way for a cost study (a survivor panel understates exit rotation), so every breakeven quoted is optimistic. Proposed RULES wording in the result file: any 4b proposal must report its cross-universe breakeven cost, and a breakeven under 15 bps is not capital-worthy without a measured execution cost. See `research/backtests/2026-09-04_cost-sensitivity_cloud.result.md`.
13. 52w-high-proximity — **KILL** (2026-09-04, lane C). 28 points (2 universes x [live-v1 + EWall control + 3 signals x n in {5,10,20,30}]), all reported; harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, halves 1.088/1.103) and idea 10's `B136/EWall` to the decimal. **Ranking on `P_t / max(P_{t-251..t})` loses to the incumbent composite in 8 of 8 matched-n pairs** (same names, days, gate and gross): dSharpe **-0.045..-0.754**, paired daily **t -3.07..-4.16**, every sign negative, mean **-7.2%/yr** (universe.json) and **-9.9%/yr** (broad); the 50/50 BLEND loses in 7 of 8. **0 of 16 PROX/BLEND points pass 4b anywhere and 0 of 28 points pass 4b on both lists.** **The signal is negatively informative inside the gate:** weekly rank IC vs next week's return over 905/914 weeks is **-0.0208 (t -2.13)** on universe.json and **-0.0252 (t -3.34)** on broad, negative in BOTH halves of BOTH lists, against COMP's +0.0427 (t +4.16) / +0.0242 (t +3.22). Mechanism: the 200d gate has already spent it — mean PROX of eligible names 0.953 vs 0.902 for all names — so what remains is a level statistic only +0.192/+0.324 Spearman-correlated with the composite, and the part that differs is the part that loses; vol falls in 8/8 pairs and MaxDD is shallower in 6/8 while CAGR falls further every time (risk fell, return fell more, as in idea 10). **It loses to no ranking at all** (vs EW-all-eligible: dSharpe -0.900..+0.004, t -4.10..-1.98 at every n on both lists, while COMP beats the same control on return in 8/8). **The whipsaw prior pointed the wrong way and that is the transferable finding:** a level key bounded near 1.0 is FASTER, not slower — at matched n on universe.json turnover is 40.2/31.1/14.8/7.8x vs COMP's 17.6/13.9/9.6/7.0x and flips/ticker/yr 4.8/7.4/7.0/5.4 vs 2.1/3.2/4.4/4.7, because names cluster within a few percent of their highs and tiny moves reshuffle the book. **Rule 8 rejects it unanimously and with power:** Spearman(IS,OOS Sharpe) **+0.888/+0.930** (vs idea 8's +0.000), S1 and S2 pick COMP-n20 (OOS 14.4%/1.170/-18.3%, clears) and COMP-n30 (OOS 11.6%/0.904/-20.3%, misses OOS DD by 0.1pp), and COMP wins 7 of 8 within-n signal audits. Nothing upgraded — the run's three 4b passes are reproductions of ideas 2, 8 and 10's published rows; 0 of 14 points pass 4a on universe.json. Survivorship flatters PROX (delisted never-near-high names are absent) and it loses anyway, so the KILL is conservative. RULES wording recommended: none. Memo: research/backtests/2026-09-04_52w-high-proximity_C.result.md. Ideas 79-80 queued.
73. asset-class-dispersion — **KILL for dispersion as a universe clause** (2026-09-04, lane B). 35 points (7 panels x {EWall, v1, CAND-5/10/20}) on a common 2011-2026 window, all reported; harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row and the live v1 row to the decimal. **The premise is true, the inference is false.** Panel dispersion of 12-1 momentum orders exactly as idea 10 assumed (ETF24 0.129 < ETF36 0.145 < B136 0.257 < BSTK100 0.278 < U56 0.281 < STK20 0.378 < SMALL484 0.682) but the net ranking premium (CAND-n minus that panel's own EW-all-eligible) has Spearman **-0.107/-0.071/-0.071** with it at n=5/10/20, and the run's largest premium is **ETF36/CAND20 +0.228 Sharpe (t +2.41)** on the second-*lowest*-dispersion panel while the widest panel (SMALL484) has the worst net Sharpe of all 21 CAND points. **The cross-panel gross spread tracks candidate count, not spread:** Spearman(n_elig, spread) +0.571/+0.857/+0.964 vs Spearman(sd, spread) +0.429/+0.679/+0.357, and the two are confounded. **The powered test is flat:** within-panel, expanding-quantile dispersion terciles (no look-ahead) pay hi>lo in only 2/2/3 of 7 panels; pooled with panel fixed effects over 4,877 panel-weeks corr = -0.006/-0.015/+0.001, |t| <= 1.01. **Rule 8 rejects it:** the pre-registered dispersion rule S3 picks SMALL484 -> OOS 7.6%/0.506/-26.3%, failing every OOS 4b bar and finishing last of seven, while S1 picks STK20/CAND10 (OOS 21.2%/1.366/-17.3% PASS) and S2 picks STK20/CAND20 (OOS 14.0%/1.449/-12.1% PASS); Spearman(IS dispersion, OOS Sharpe) +0.134 against Spearman(IS Sharpe, OOS Sharpe) **+0.710** — the panel choice is predictable, dispersion just is not what predicts it. **Idea 10's ETF KILL is re-explained:** ETFs fail on the *level* of return (ETF36/CAND20 6.6% CAGR vs the 9.91% 4b floor) with the best ranking premium in the run, so ranking is not what breaks there. Nothing upgraded (the only 4b passes are idea 2's U56 books and idea 10's STK20 PARK); survivorship falls hardest on the high-dispersion panels, biasing the run toward the hypothesis it rejects. RULES wording recommended: none. Memo: research/backtests/2026-09-04_asset-class-dispersion_B.result.md. Ideas 77-78 queued.
73b. asset-class-dispersion (INDEPENDENT SECOND RUN, cloud lane — lane B ran the same idea the same day; both reach KILL) — **KILL as a rule the project can act on; hypothesis CONFIRMED as a composition property and REJECTED as a timing signal.** 7 panels x 7 arms x 3 costs = 147 points plus 150 random sub-panel draws, all reported; harness reproduces ideas 2, 28 and 10's published rows to the decimal (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, 10.4%/1.050/-15.9%, 6.8%/0.817/-15.2%) and the analytic cost model is bit-identical to the engine. **A confound had to be removed first, and finding it is half the result:** idea 2's literal `GROSS/n` book is not a ranking on a panel with fewer than n eligible names — STK20 averages 13.2 eligible, so its `CAND-n20` holds *every* eligible name at 3.75% each, i.e. the unranked book at **49.2% gross**, and its apparent "+0.090 ranking premium" is de-grossing (idea 66). Gross-normalising the ranked book (`CANDg-n`: top-n equal-weighted to the same 75%) takes STK20's n=20 premium from +0.090 to **-0.003** and n=10 from +0.057 to -0.004. Every number below uses the gross-matched contrast. **(A) Across panels, nothing:** Spearman(mean dispersion, premium) over the 7 panels is -0.21..+0.46 across 4 dispersion measures x 3 n, and **not one of the 12 has |t| > 1.2**. **(B) Across time, nothing — this is the decisive negative.** Sorting each panel's weeks into quintiles of its own trailing dispersion (known at the prior close), the ranked-minus-unranked excess is **monotone in 0 of 14 (panel, measure) cells, Q5-Q1 is positive in only 3 of 7 panels, and |t| > 2 in 0 of 14**; mean Q5-Q1 +0.05pp/yr on disp_std. The panel-year regression looks supportive pooled (+8.28pp per unit, t +2.27, R2 0.040) and **collapses under panel fixed effects to t +1.09, R2 0.010** — the pooled slope was the between-panel variation test A already showed is uninformative. So dispersion is not a regime signal and cannot be traded. **(C) Across composition, real:** 150 random 36-name sub-panels of B136 (asset class, sample, gate and gross all held fixed; only *which* names vary) give slope **+0.617 Sharpe per unit of dispersion, t +3.69**, Spearman +0.297 (t +3.78), top-vs-bottom tercile +0.044 (t +3.72). Its extrapolated break-even, **disp_std = 0.362**, is fitted only on B136 draws and **predicts the sign of the ranking premium on 6 of the 7 panels out of sample** (the miss is STK20 at 0.378, predicted +0.009 vs actual -0.004 — two ties either side of the line, which is what a break-even should look like). **Why it still changes nothing:** the premium is **negative in 132 of 150 draws** (mean -0.072) and in **16 of 21 (panel, n) cells**, so over every dispersion level the project can actually trade, ranking subtracts value — dispersion only makes it less bad. The two panels above break-even are the two that cannot be used: STK20 fails 4b on drawdown (-30.8% gross-matched at every n) and SMALL439 fails all five bars (0.474 Sharpe vs SPY 0.863). And the honest control kills the interpretation: dispersion predicts BOTH books' Sharpe far more strongly than the gap between them (CANDg level slope +2.582, t +10.58, R2 0.431; EWall +1.965, t +8.46, R2 0.326), so **only 24% of dispersion's effect is differential** — it is mostly a proxy for "this panel went up", which on current-constituent lists is exactly what survivorship manufactures. **Rule 8 cannot use it either:** IS ranking premium vs OOS ranking premium is Spearman **+0.086 (t +0.38)** over 21 (panel, n) points; Rule A picks B136/CANDg-n10 and FAILS OOS 4b, Rule B picks U56/CANDg-n20 and clears (OOS 14.5%/1.132/-18.3%) — the same n=20 large-cap arm the project already has. **Consequence for RULES:** no dispersion floor should be written, because the only measurable version of it (composition) is unusable at the levels available and the actionable version (timing) does not exist; what the run does support is the weaker existing clause — a ranked book needs the eligible set to be materially larger than n, or it is an unranked book at reduced gross. Survivorship: current constituents on all 7 panels, worst on STK20 and SMALL439; it runs *toward* the dispersion result (the levels regression) and so weakens it, and the premium contrasts hold names/days/gate/gross fixed. **Agreement and one correction vs lane B's run (entry above):** both runs independently reach the same verdict and the same decisive negative — lane B's pooled panel-fixed-effect correlation is -0.006/-0.015/+0.001 with |t| <= 1.01, this run's panel-FE slope is t +1.09, R2 0.010, so dispersion does not time in either harness, on different panel definitions and different quantile schemes. **The correction:** lane B's headline counter-example — 'the run's largest premium is ETF36/CAND20 +0.228 on the second-lowest-dispersion panel' — is substantially the de-grossing artefact identified here. ETF36 averages 24.3 eligible names, so `CAND-n20` there runs below full gross; this run reproduces the literal figure (+0.187 on its window) and the gross-matched contrast is **+0.042**. Removing that artefact deletes the strongest evidence AGAINST the dispersion hypothesis, which is why this run reaches a partly different reading of the mechanism (real across composition, t +3.69) while reaching the identical conclusion for RULES: no dispersion clause. Panel definitions also differ — lane B's SMALL484 keeps the 44 tickers with max_1d_move >= 1.0 (dispersion 0.682); this run drops them per data/SMALL_PANEL_README.md (SMALL439, dispersion 0.491). Ideas 81-83 queued (2026-09-04, cloud)
9. trailing-stop — **KILL as a source of Sharpe, plus the cheapest drawdown instrument the project has measured.** 66 arms (2 universes x 3 books x [5 stop depths x 2 cooldowns + no-stop control]) x 3 costs, all reported; the stop simulator reproduces engine.backtest to 0.00e+00 on returns AND turnover with the stop disabled, and ideas 2 and 57's published rows to the decimal. **The literal queued idea is a no-op:** a 15% trailing stop on RULES v1 fires **0.4 times a year** — 7 times in 17.6 years — because v1's `/sqrt(vol20)` scaler selects low-vol names (mean vol20 of held names 0.144 vs top20's 0.228) and holds them a mean 16 days, so the price rarely gets far enough from its running high; it moves v1 from 6.5%/0.666/-13.8% to 6.3%/0.654/**-14.1%**, i.e. it makes drawdown *worse*. At S=20% and deeper the stop never fires at all on v1 and every column is bit-identical to the control. An independent episode audit (walks holding runs of the unstopped book, never touches the simulator) confirms the rate: v1 hit rate 3.3% of episodes vs top20's 22.4% and ew-band3's 70.3%. **On books where it does bite, it loses in 22 of 22 arms on universe.json and 22 of 24 on broad** (the 2 exceptions are v1/S10 on broad, +0.010/+0.014 at t +0.60): dSharpe vs the same book with no stop is -0.004..-0.096 at 10 bps and -0.008..-0.131 at 25, and worse at 25 in every pair — the whipsaw ordering of ideas 55/57/4 extended to the fastest instrument yet tested. **The depth dial is monotone and points at zero:** net Sharpe rises with stop depth toward `none` in 6 of 6 (book, cooldown) rows on universe.json and 10 of 12 across both lists, so this is a gradient, not noise. **The one real finding is the exchange rate.** As insurance the stop is far cheaper than the incumbent 200d gate: it buys drawdown at **0.17-0.53 pp of CAGR per pp of MaxDD** in the top20 book (S20/C0: 1.35pp of drawdown for 0.23pp of CAGR) against idea 57's measured **4.45 pp per pp** for the 200d gate — an order of magnitude better — and at MATCHED MaxDD (the no-stop book de-grossed by bisection until its drawdown equals the stop arm's, valid because idea 66 showed gross is an exact lever) the stop delivers **more CAGR than holding less in 8 of 8 top20 arms that bite** (+0.20 to +1.62pp). It still loses Sharpe to that control in **30 of 30** arms, so it is a way to *hit a drawdown target*, never a way to earn one. **Nothing is upgraded:** 6 of 33 arms pass 4b on both universes; one of the six is the no-stop control itself and 4 of the remaining 5 are S25/S30 near-no-ops sitting within 0.01 Sharpe of it; the only biting arm to pass both (ew-band3/S20/C0, 11.0%/1.129/-14.8% and 10.9%/1.063/-16.7%) is strictly worse than the no-stop candidate it overlays on both lists. The three 4a passes on universe.json (top20/S10/C21, ew-band3/S10/C21, ew-band3/S15/C21) are bought by giving up 1.2-3.2pp of CAGR to get under the live book's -13.8% drawdown, and each **fails 4b on CAGR** — adopting one would trade a 4b candidate for a 4a one. 4a on the broad list is not a bar at all (v1 itself draws down -21.2% there, so nearly every arm clears it trivially and 4a should be read on universe.json only). **Rule 8 rejects the overlay on 3 of 4 selections:** universe.json Rule A picks ew-band3/S15/C21 (OOS 10.6%/1.176/-12.8%, FAILS OOS 4b on CAGR), Rule B picks top20/S20/C0 (OOS 14.0%/1.162/-17.0%, clears — and it is the arm nearest to no-stop); broad picks ew-band3/S15/C21 and S20/C0, both FAIL OOS 4b; and the IS-chosen stop matches the OOS-best in 2 of 6 books. Survivorship: current constituents, one-directional; the stop-vs-no-stop pairs hold names, signal and days fixed and are much less exposed. Ideas 74-76 queued (2026-09-04, cloud)
10. sector-only-universe — **KILL** (2026-09-04, lane C). 0 of 10 ETF-only points pass 4a or 4b; best ETF arm `ETF36/CAND-n20` 6.8%/0.817/-15.2% (halves 0.754/0.878, OOS 0.943) fails 4b on H1 and CAGR (6.8% vs the 10.68% floor), the literal queued arm `ETF24` 6.7%/0.779/-15.2% fails H1, H2 and CAGR. **Half the hypothesis holds and does not help:** matched construction/days/gross, ETF36 has lower vol in 10 of 10 pairs and ETF24 in 6 of 10, but MaxDD is shallower in only 4/10 and 2/10 and **Sharpe is worse in 20 of 20** — -6.15%/yr (t to -5.31) and -5.02%/yr (t to -4.11). Risk fell, return fell more. **The complement is the finding:** the same list stripped to its 20 mega-cap stocks gives the run's only 4a pass (`STK20/CAND-n20` 12.1%/1.338/-12.1%, halves 1.341/1.344, OOS 14.0%/1.449/-12.1%), passes 4b, and **both rule-8 rules select a STK20 arm and clear every OOS bar** (Spearman IS/OOS Sharpe +0.608, the project's best). **PARK not KEEP, on the run's own control:** the stock-leg advantage is +5.19%/yr (t to +4.45) on 20 curated names but only +0.92%/yr (t max +2.04) on universe_broad.json's 100 stocks, which fail 4b on drawdown at every n — a 5.6x shrink with a wider leg is selection, not a premium, and these 20 tickers are the most survivorship-exposed panel in the repo. Memo written recommending rejection with the exact RULES wording. Also: the 36 ETFs are bit-identical across both universes (symmetric difference []), so an ETF book passes idea 53's cross-universe test by construction and still fails 4b — universe invariance is not a substitute for return; and `B136/EWall` (no ranking at all) passes 4b at 10.7%/1.027/-17.7%. Ideas 71-73 queued.
66. gross-exposure-is-the-error — **KILL** (2026-09-04, lane B). Gross is an exact lever: corr 1.0000 / beta=g / dSharpe 0.000 across 36 arms x 6 costs x 2 universes; broad top20 H2 moves 0.814->0.815 from g=0.75 to 1.00 while MaxDD deepens 6.1pp, so gross cannot fix an H2 Sharpe bar. Both rule-8 rules overshoot on gross and FAIL OOS drawdown on both universes. The 25% core is not a detour — it is the only lever that touches a Sharpe bar (+0.047 broad H2, t<1) — but it is worse than all-active at matched investment on universe.json. PARK: ew-band3 g=0.90 passes 4b cross-universe at 5-25 bps but rule 8 cannot select it.
68. lookback-candidate-cost-and-lag — **KILL idea 8's `12-1 n=30` PARK** (2026-09-04, cloud). 80 points (2 universes x 2 signals x n in {20,30} x cost in {5,10,15,25,50} bps x lag in {1d,1w}), all reported; harness reproduces idea 8's published rows on both lists to the decimal and the analytic cost model is bit-identical to the engine (max |diff| 0.00e+00). **The cross-universe 4b pass survives only at 5 and 10 bps with next-day execution and is lost at EVERY cost under a 1-week lag**; at 1d it breaks between 10 and 15 bps, on both lists at once but for different reasons (u.json runs out of CAGR, broad out of H2 Sharpe). **The 10 bps pass was never real:** the margins are **+0.188pp of CAGR** on u.json (10.868% vs the 10.680% floor) and **+0.059pp of drawdown** on broad (-20.171% vs the -20.230% cap) — each list passes on one rounding-level tie. **The pre-registered hypothesis is confirmed and does not save it:** at 6.70x turnover the candidate decays at -0.068 Sharpe/10bps vs the incumbent blend-v1 n=20's -0.084 (u.json; -0.083 vs -0.099 on broad) and beats it on Sharpe by +0.066/+0.110 at 50 bps despite losing -1.94%/yr (t -2.62) at 5 bps — but lower turnover buys exactly **one cost step** (incumbent's cross-universe pass dies at 10 bps, the candidate's at 15), nowhere near the 25 bps idea 63's core-sleeve arm reached. **New finding — a week's execution delay costs risk, not return:** across all 4 books x 2 universes the 1w-minus-1d return difference is **+0.18..+0.47%/yr with every paired t in +0.27..+0.55 and every sign positive**, while broad MaxDD deepens 2.5pp (candidate -20.17% -> -22.70%), H2 falls 0.875 -> 0.787 and OOS 0.971 -> 0.883. A stale book takes the same crashes a week later and larger. **Rule 8 selects the candidate only where it fails OOS:** 0 of 20 selections on u.json; on broad plain-Sharpe picks it in 6 of 10 cells (all 1w-lag or 50 bps) and **all 6 miss the OOS 4b bars** (OOS DD -22.6..-23.6%), while the 4b-aware rule picks it 0 times and finds nothing at all in 6 of 10. Survivorship: current constituents; the cost/lag comparisons hold names/days/gate/gross fixed. Nothing queued — the PARK is closed, and idea 45/47/58 already cover the same protocol for the other candidates.
6. defensive-sleeve — **KILL for the breadth trigger** (2026-09-04, cloud). 44 points (2 universes x 2 books x [B in {30,40,50,60}% x L in {63,126}d + B=0 plain + B=100 always-on]) plus 16 sleeve-definition controls, all reported; harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row exactly. **The trigger is indistinguishable from zero**: paired daily t vs the same book with the sleeve off is -0.25..+0.60 across all 40 triggered arms, the sign flips between adjacent B values and between universes, and the Sharpe delta is negative in **all 16** triggered top20 arms. **It fails in the year it was built for** — at B=40 the trigger is on 71% of 2022 days (u.json) / 57% (broad) and 2022 is its worst year: top20 B=40 **-14.1% vs the plain book's -9.0%** (u.json) and -14.0% vs -10.9% (broad), because TLT and GLD fell with equities; its biggest gain (2020 +25.6% vs +15.4%) is GLD's rally, which the always-on control captures with no trigger at all. **Two clean negatives:** "best-of-3 by momentum" loses to naive equal-weight of the three sleeve assets in 4 of 4 (book, universe) cells and to SHY-only in 3 of 4, and L=126 loses to L=63 in 13 of 16 matched pairs. **It damages the standing candidate:** 7 of 8 triggered top20 arms on u.json fail 4b and all 7 fail on **H1 alone** (1.088 -> 0.911-0.984), because 2009-11 is the other low-breadth window and the sleeve sat in bonds through the recovery; turnover 9.6x -> 11.6x for a sleeve on 13% of days. Only 1 of 40 triggered arms passes 4b (u.json top20 B=30/L=63) at a *lower* Sharpe than the plain book it overlays, and it fails broad on drawdown. **Rule 8 rejects the overlay 4 of 4**: the plain-Sharpe rule picks B=0 every time, the 4b-aware rule picks NOTHING in 3 of 4. Survivorship: current constituents; the overlay-vs-plain comparison holds names/days/gross fixed. Nothing queued — the one live question it raises (the always-on cash replacement is +1.2..+1.7pp CAGR at t<1.6) is a gross-exposure question already covered by idea 66.
8. lookback-blend — **KILL for changing the ranking horizon** (2026-09-04, lane C). 20 grid points (5 signals x n in {5,10,20,30}) on two universes, all reported; harness reproduces idea 2's KEEP row exactly. No component beats the incumbent blend at the candidate's position count: paired daily t vs blend-v1 at n=20/30 is -1.80..-0.43 on universe.json and +0.74..+1.40 on broad — nothing clears |t|=2 either way. **The walk-forward cannot select a horizon at all on the primary list: Spearman(IS Sharpe, OOS Sharpe) = +0.000** across the 20 points (+0.463 on broad); the IS-best 6-1 n=5 misses OOS 4b on drawdown (-22.8%), the 4b-aware pick 6-1 n=20 clears it on universe.json (12.3%/1.020/-19.2%) but the same rule on broad picks 6-1 n=30 and misses by 0.4pp. **Two keepers:** (a) **3-1 is the harmful leg** — weekly rank IC +0.0224 (t 2.07) on universe.json and +0.0110 (t 1.40) on broad vs 12-1's +0.0497 (t 4.29) and blend-v1's +0.0499 (t 4.74), costing -2.11%/yr (t -3.17) at n=20; blend-v1 is the most stable leg, the only signal whose IC does not decay H1->H2 (+0.046 -> +0.054). (b) **12-1 n=30 is the only point passing 4b on BOTH universes** (u.json 10.9%/1.097/-15.8%, OOS 1.193, turn 6.7x; broad 13.0%/1.004/-20.2%, OOS 0.971, also 4a) — but rule 8 never selects it, and its broad drawdown sits exactly on the cap, so **PARK not KEEP**; memo written. Ranking earns its keep only in concentrated books (vs unranked EW-all-eligible: n=5 t +2.4..+3.3, n=30 t +0.1..+0.7), and on u.json n=30 holds 27.6 of a mean 37.5 eligible names so it is nearly the unranked control. Sign checks pass (reversed books 0.20-0.35 Sharpe; 1m reversal IC -0.007). Survivorship: current constituents; signal-vs-signal comparisons hold names/days/gate/gross fixed. Idea 68 queued (2026-09-04, lane C)
67. core-sleeve-walk-forward-repair — KILL the repair (2026-09-04, lane B): 0 of 24 IS selection points (2 objectives x 6 drawdown caps x 2 universes) pick b=0.25; 10/12 on broad pick b=0.50 and fail OOS DD by 1.8pp. IS Sharpe ranks OOS MaxDD at -0.19/-0.45 and IS MaxDD ranks OOS MaxDD at +0.57/+0.20, so no IS drawdown cap can discriminate. The QQQ core, not b, breaks rule 8 (SPY core: 10/12 points pass OOS 4b on broad). Surviving arm: ew-band3 + 0-25% SPY core.
63. broad-H2-binding-bar — **HYPOTHESIS REJECTED (the H2 shortfall is not mega-cap concentration and is not one year), plus a 4b KEEP-candidate that falls out of the diagnosis.** H2 = 2017-11-03 to 2026-09-03; harness reproduces the published broad rows exactly. **(a) Premise corrected:** on broad at 10 bps the equal-weight books ALREADY clear the H2 bar (ew-all 0.917, ew-band3 0.971 vs SPY 0.837); only the ranked top20 fails it (0.814) — idea 4's "9 of 10 bind on H2 or MaxDD" mixes two different failures. **(b) Not concentration:** regressing daily excess over SPY on the tradable factor RSP-SPY, H2 betas are +0.04..+0.22 with **R2 0.011-0.018** and the entire shortfall sits in alpha (-3.7 to -9.2%/yr), while in H1 the same books load NEGATIVELY (beta -0.92..-1.74, R2 0.15-0.26) — a momentum book already holds the mega-caps. **(c) Not one year:** dropping 2019, 2021 OR 2023 individually flips broad top20's H2 test; the worst drag (2019, t -2.22) had RSP-SPY at only -2.3pp while 2024's -12.1pp of concentration hurt less. The books beat SPY in exactly two H2 years, 2018 and 2022. **The mechanism is 75% gross in a half where SPY compounded 15.0%/yr** (books 9.7-11.5%). **(d) Remedy + control:** replacing 25% of gross with a passive core (matched 75% gross) takes cross-universe 4b passes from 1 of 4 books to 3 of 4 — best arm **ew-band3 + 25% core: universe.json 12.4%/1.142/-16.2%, broad 12.3%/1.086/-18.5%, passing 4b on both lists at 5, 10 AND 25 bps with a QQQ core** (the project's first arm to survive 25 bps cross-universe). The honest control: a plain **SPY or VTI core fixes the same bar** (broad top20 H2 0.814 -> 0.861/0.853), so the fix is beta, not the NASDAQ; QQQ's only edge is the 25 bps pass, which is the hindsight premium. **Weakness:** rule 8 picks b=0.50 on both universes — OOS clears on universe.json (15.4%/1.106/-18.8%) but FAILS broad OOS drawdown (-22.0% vs -20.2%); b=0.25 is selected by the sample-wide 4b test, not the walk-forward. Survivorship cuts against the rejected hypothesis (the mega-caps are in the list because they won) which strengthens it. Memo written. Ideas 66-67 queued (2026-09-04, cloud)
3. rebalance-freq — **KILL for daily and quarterly, PARK for monthly, robustness PASS for idea 57's candidate.** 128 points (2 universes x 4 books x 4 cadences x 4 costs), all reported; harness reproduces ideas 2, 57 and the ew-all control to the decimal and the analytic cost model is bit-identical to the engine. **Daily is worse than weekly in 8 of 8 (universe, book) cells** — dSharpe -0.00..-0.40, t -1.6 to **-4.6** at 10 bps and -1.7 to -8.8 at 25 bps — so "do not trade more often than weekly" is the one unambiguous result. **Monthly beats weekly in 6 of 8 cells and the gap widens with cost** (v1 +3.72pp CAGR/+0.295 Sharpe **t +2.64** on universe.json; top20 +2.05pp/+0.111 t +2.10 and +3.24pp/+0.143 t +2.52 on broad), exactly as the whipsaw story predicts — **but it buys return with drawdown**: `top20/M` is the best cell the project has produced on universe.json (14.7%/1.204/-19.5%, halves 1.211/1.206, OOS 1.286, passing 4b at every cost 5-50 bps, a first) and fails 4b on the broad list by 5.9pp of MaxDD (-26.1% vs the -20.2% cap); every monthly and quarterly arm fails 4b's DD cap on broad. Quarterly cannot re-enter after a crash (2020: top20 Q +8.1%/+1.0% vs M +22.9%/+14.0%). **Cross-universe 4b: 2 of 16 arms pass on both lists, both of them idea 57's `ew-band3` (at D and W)** — and that book is also the least cadence-sensitive anywhere (dSharpe -0.03..+0.00 across D/W/M), which is real evidence its edge is not a weekly-calendar artefact. **Rule 8 rejects every cadence change:** universe.json picks ew-all/Q under both rules (OOS 11.7%/1.044/-22.2%, fails OOS DD and CAGR), broad picks top20/M (OOS 15.7%/1.009/-26.1%, fails OOS DD), and the IS-chosen cadence matches the OOS-best in only 2 of 8 books. Survivorship: current constituents, one-directional; cadence-vs-cadence comparisons hold names and days fixed and are much less exposed. Ideas 64-65 queued (2026-09-04, cloud)
4. abs-momentum-filter — **KILL** for 12-1 absolute momentum as a replacement for, or an addition to, the 200d-MA gate. 80 points (2 books x 5 gates x 4 costs x 2 universes), all reported; harness reproduces ideas 2 and 57 to the decimal and the analytic cost model is bit-identical to the engine. **The mechanism prediction held and the payoff did not:** abs IS the slower signal (5.75 flips/tkr/yr vs the 200d's 7.55, 6.4x vs 8.2x turnover), but vs the incumbent it is only +0.94pp CAGR/+0.021 Sharpe (t +0.92) on universe.json and +1.05pp/+0.040 (t +1.19) on broad in the gate-only ew-all book, and NEGATIVE in the ranked top20 book on both lists (-0.035, -0.014); vs the ungated control its Sharpe delta is negative in 3 of 4 cells. **The AND arm is the run's clear loser** — stricter and faster (8.06 flips, 9.7x turnover, 33 names), -1.36pp CAGR/-0.067 Sharpe vs the incumbent on universe.json top20 (**t -1.99**), -2.41pp/-0.096 vs no gate (t -2.50). **Two arms nominally pass 4b on both universes but by ties, not margins:** top20+both clears broad H2 by **+0.0002** and ew-all+abs clears broad DD by **+0.12pp**; both die by 25 bps, and idea 57's band3 remains the only arm with real slack (H2 +0.134). **All four walk-forward selections reject idea 4** — universe.json picks top20/none under both rules (OOS 14.9%/1.164/-18.5%), broad picks ew-all/none (Rule A, fails OOS DD) and ew-all/band3 (Rule B, OOS 11.2%/1.074/-16.8%) vs SPY 15.5%/0.884/-33.7% and RULES v1 7.8%/0.751/-13.8%. **New finding:** the whipsaw ordering (fewer flips -> better net Sharpe) is monotone in the gate-only book on universe.json and near-monotone on broad, but absent or inverted in the ranked top20 book — gate choice is a real decision only where the gate is the only thing happening. Survivorship: current constituents, one-directional; the gate-vs-gate comparisons much less exposed. Ideas 61-63 queued (2026-09-04, lane C)
57. trend-gate-as-drawdown-insurance — **KILL for the incumbent weekly 200d gate as an insurance instrument (it never pays), plus a 4b KEEP-candidate for the band that replaces it — the project's first arm to clear 4b on BOTH large-cap universes.** 2 books x 5 gate instruments x 4 cost levels = 40 points per universe, all reported; harness reproduces ideas 2 and 55 to the decimal and the analytic cost model is bit-identical to the engine. **(a) The 200d gate's Sharpe delta vs the ungated book is negative at every cost on both books and degrades monotonically:** on top20 it costs 1.06pp CAGR for 0.24pp of MaxDD (4.45pp per pp, dSharpe -0.030 at 10bps); on the ew-all control it costs 1.81pp CAGR / -0.077 Sharpe for 2.51pp of drawdown at 10 bps, and at 50 bps gives up 4.55pp CAGR while the drawdown benefit itself collapses to +0.30pp. Paired: -1.00%/yr t -1.61 (top20) and -2.06%/yr **t -2.13** (ew-all, broad). **(b) Cheaper instruments are strictly better insurance:** the gate flips 7.55x/ticker/yr vs 1.69x monthly and 1.77x for a 3% band; at 10 bps on ew-all the band buys MORE drawdown (+3.25pp vs +2.51pp) for LESS CAGR (-0.96 vs -1.81) at 4.9x turnover vs 8.2x, and is the only instrument anywhere with a non-negative Sharpe delta (+0.009 at 10bps, +0.025 at 5). Mechanism is whipsaw: at top20, 2020 reads none +13.8%, 200d +15.4%, monthly +18.1%; 2022 none -8.9%, 200d -9.0%, monthly -6.8%. **KEEP-candidate: ew-all + band3** — universe.json 11.3%/1.136/-15.1% (halves 1.113/1.160, OOS 12.6%/1.234), broad 11.1%/1.064/-16.8% (halves 1.163/0.971, OOS 11.2%/1.074), 4.9x turnover; 3 of 10 arms pass 4b on both lists at 10 bps (all three are ew-all + a cheap gate; the incumbent 200d and every top20 arm fail), which fixes idea 28's 0.23pp CAGR miss and the universe-fragility that kills ideas 2 and 55. **Honest limits:** universe.json's walk-forward picks top20/none instead (broad's 4b-aware rule does pick band3); at 25 bps nothing passes both universes (5/10, 0/10), at 50 bps 1/10 and 0/10. Survivorship: current constituents, one-directional; the gate-vs-gate comparisons are much less exposed. Memo written. Ideas 58-60 queued (2026-09-04, lane B)
55. trend-gate-lookback-primary — **KILL for the trend gate as a source of edge on the primary universe, plus one 4b KEEP-candidate.** Idea 2's construction with K in {200d,100d,50d,none} x n in {5,10,20,30}, all 16 points reported; harness check reproduces idea 2's KEEP row to the decimal (12.7%/1.093/-18.3%). **K=none beats K=200d at every n on universe.json** — n=20 reads 13.7%/1.123/-18.5% (halves 1.154/1.101, OOS 1.164) vs the incumbent's 12.7%/1.093/-18.3%, same 9.5x turnover — and **both** pre-registered walk-forward rules pick K=none n=20, OOS 14.9%/1.164/-18.5% clearing every OOS 4b bar vs SPY 15.5%/0.884/-33.7%. 6 of 16 pass 4b, 1 passes 4a. **But the gate's contribution is statistically indistinguishable from zero**: dropping 200d is +0.35..+1.47%/yr (t +1.42..+1.76) on universe.json and -0.22..+0.19%/yr (t -0.50..+0.68) on the broad list — sign unstable between two overlapping large-cap lists. Only the **50d** gate is decisively harmful (+3.0..+5.5%/yr from dropping it, t +2.6..+3.9, on BOTH universes). Gate-only control (EW all eligible, no ranking), consistent in sign on both lists: the 200d gate costs ~1.8-2.2pp CAGR and ~0.09 Sharpe (t +1.47 / **t +2.13**) and **6.4x/yr of turnover** to buy 2.5-3.1pp of drawdown — drawdown insurance, not alpha. By year at n=20 the gate helped only in 2020 (+1.6pp) and was a dead heat in 2022 (-9.0% vs -8.9%). **0 of 16 broad points pass 4b** and the two universes' walk-forwards pick opposite arms, so the candidate inherits idea 2's universe fragility exactly. Survivorship: current constituents, one-directional; the K-vs-K comparisons are much less exposed. Memo written. Ideas 56-57 queued (2026-09-04, cloud)

35. options-iv-snapshot-cache — **BUILT, accruing**: `research/options_cache.py` snapshots the two nearest monthly expiries for 96 tickers (56 universe ex-crypto + 40 deep-value candidates) into `data/options/iv_YYYY-MM-DD.csv` and `data/options/iv_panel.csv`; runs in 6s and is now a step in daily.yml. First snapshot 2026-09-04: 93/96 tickers, 186 rows. **yfinance's option quotes are largely broken**: Yahoo's `impliedVolatility` is a failed-solve ladder (0.0625*2^-k) and bid/ask is 0 across every strike for SPY, AAPL and NVDA even during regular hours. The script therefore solves its own Black-Scholes IV, mid -> lastPrice -> Yahoo, and records `iv_src`/`n_quoted`/`stale_days` per row. Post-fix sanity: SPY ATM 11.9%, QQQ 16.6%, TSLA 52.8%, corr(ATM IV, RV20) = 0.60, median IV-RV spread +4.3pp. No test possible until a few months of panel exist (2026-09-04, local data agents)
